respectively.

and

having non-zero coordinates set to

and

respectively. We compare (
3
) (

) with sparse regression with Slope norm regularization (“Slope reg”). See Figure

(b).

) with

,

,

. The corruption vector

is set to have the first

and zero otherwise. See Figure

(a).

,

. This time, we compare “Huber” and “Sorted Huber”. See Figure

and

13
Discussion

We present theoretical and empirical improvements resorting to “sorted” variations of the classical Huber’s loss. Instead of the
\ell_{1}
norm, the loss in Definition
1
is based on a variational formula regularized by the slope norm — treating each label outlier magnitude individually. This use of the slope norm refines an existing robust loss and should be contrasted to
[
11
,
6
]
. There, the slope norm is used as a refined regularization norm when estimating the sparse parameter.
23
23

23

While the estimator (
3
) of
[\boldsymbol{b}^{*},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}]
can be written as a slope estimator with the augmented design
[\mathbb{X}^{(n)}-\mathbf{I}_{n}]
, this point of view is not enough to entail the optimal rate. Indeed,

\IP
is crucially needed to entail optimality when estimating only
\boldsymbol{b}^{*}
. See Remarks
9
-
10
and comments after Proposition
3
.
More generally, this work identifies three design properties,

\PP
,
\IP
and
\MP
, which jointly entail that the robust estimator (
3
) is near-optimal in terms of dimension,
\epsilon
and
\delta
— when dealing simultaneously with matrix decomposition, label contamination, featured-dependent noise and miss-specification. These properties are based on sharp concentration inequalities stated in Section
7
. We believe these could be useful elsewhere, e.g., non-parametric least squares regression
[
46
,
54
]
and compressive sensing theory
[
37
]
. We reemphasize that there seems to be no prior estimation theory for RTRMD — with
\PP
being a new application of the product process inequality. Under the incoherence assumption, it would be interesting to investigate further when exact recovery is possible or how nonconvex optimization approaches behave on the model RTRMD
[
20
]
.

In this supplement we give detailed proofs of the results stated in the main text. It is organized as follows:

to

.

Proof of Lemma
9

From
\PP_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{R}}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4})
,

\displaystyle|\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}^{2}-\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{1}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}+(\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}+\alpha_{4}\mathcal{R}^{2}(\mathbf{V})

(101)

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}\left(\alpha_{1}+(\nicefrac{{\alpha^{2}}}{{2}})\right)+\mathcal{R}^{2}(\mathbf{V})\left(\alpha_{4}+\frac{(\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}}\right).

(102)

Let
0<\alpha<\sqrt{2(1-\alpha_{1})}
. Rearranging and taking the square-root, it follows that
\TP_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2})
holds with constants

\mathsf{a}_{1}:=\{1-\alpha_{1}-(\nicefrac{{\alpha^{2}}}{{2}})\}^{1/2}

and

\mathsf{a}_{2}:=\{\frac{(\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}}+\alpha_{4}\}^{1/2}.

The proof is finished once we set
\alpha^{2}:=(1-\alpha_{1})/2
.

Proof of Lemma
11

Let
\alpha:=(\mathsf{a}_{1}\wedge\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1})/2\sqrt{2}
and

\beta:=\mathsf{b}_{1}+\mathsf{c}_{1}
. By assumption
b:=\beta+2\alpha^{2}<(\mathsf{a}_{1}\wedge\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1})^{2}
and

\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}=(\mathsf{a}_{1}\wedge\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1})^{2}-b
. From
\TP
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{a}_{1}^{2}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}+\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1}^{2}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}-b\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}

(103)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}+\mathsf{a}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\right)^{2}+\left(\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{W})\|_{2}+\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2}\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})\right)^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}

(104)

\displaystyle-b\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}^{2},

(105)

so, after taking the square-root,
24
24

24

Here we used the relation

\sqrt{(A+B)^{2}+(\bar{A}+\bar{B})^{2}+C^{2}}\leq\sqrt{A^{2}+\bar{A}^{2}+C^{2}}+B+\bar{B}

for positive numbers
(A,\bar{A},B,\bar{B},C)
.

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{1}\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{W})\|_{2}^{2}+\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}-b\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}\right)_{+}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

(106)

\displaystyle+\mathsf{a}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})+\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2}\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W}).

(107)

In what follows, it is enough to consider the case

\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}\geq b\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}.

Taking the squares,

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{W})\|_{2}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{W})+\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}

(108)

\displaystyle-2\langle\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{W})\rangle-2\langle\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{W}),\boldsymbol{u}\rangle.

(109)

By
\IP
and Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle T_{1}:=-2\langle\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{W}),\boldsymbol{u}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathsf{b}_{1}\left\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\right\|_{\Pi}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}+2\mathsf{b}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}

(110)

\displaystyle+2\mathsf{b}_{3}\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{V})\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}+2\mathsf{b}_{4}\left\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\right\|_{\Pi}\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u})

(111)

\displaystyle\leq(\mathsf{b}_{1}+\alpha^{2})(\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2})+\frac{\mathsf{b}_{2}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\mathcal{R}^{2}(\mathbf{V})

(112)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathsf{b}_{3}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\mathcal{S}^{2}(\mathbf{W})+\frac{\mathsf{b}_{4}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\mathcal{Q}^{2}(\boldsymbol{u}).

(113)

Let
T_{2}:=-2\langle\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}),\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{W})\rangle
. By
\PP
and Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle T_{2}+2\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathsf{c}_{1}\left\|\mathbf{V}\right\|_{\Pi}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}+2\mathsf{c}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}+2\mathsf{c}_{3}\left\|\mathbf{V}\right\|_{\Pi}\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})

(114)

\displaystyle+2\gamma\mathsf{c}_{2}\mathsf{c}_{3}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})

(115)

\displaystyle\leq(\mathsf{c}_{1}+\alpha^{2})(\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}+\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}^{2})

(116)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\mathsf{c}_{2}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}+\gamma\mathsf{c}_{2}^{2}\right)\mathcal{R}^{2}(\mathbf{V})+\left(\frac{\mathsf{c}_{3}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}+\gamma\mathsf{c}_{3}^{2}\right)\mathcal{S}^{2}(\mathbf{W}).

(117)

Let
T_{0}:=\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{W})\|_{2}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}
. We thus conclude that

\displaystyle T_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u})\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}

(118)

\displaystyle+(\mathsf{b}_{1}+\mathsf{c}_{1}+2\alpha^{2})\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}+(\mathsf{b}_{1}+\alpha^{2})\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{2}

(119)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\mathsf{b}_{2}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{c}_{2}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}+\gamma\mathsf{c}_{2}^{2}\right)\mathcal{R}^{2}(\mathbf{V})+\left(\frac{\mathsf{b}_{3}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{c}_{3}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}+\gamma\mathsf{c}_{3}^{2}\right)\mathcal{S}^{2}(\mathbf{W})+\frac{\mathsf{b}_{4}^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\mathcal{Q}^{2}(\boldsymbol{u}).

(120)

From (

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{1}\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{T_{0}-b\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}^{2}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathsf{a}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})+\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2}\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})

(121)

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\|\mathfrak{M}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u})\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}\right\}_{+}^{\frac{1}{2}}

(122)

(123)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathsf{b}_{4}}{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u}).

Lemma 22
.

Proposition 4

(
\PP
)
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{X}
is
L
-subgaussian. For all
\delta\in(0,1)

and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, with probability at least

1-\delta
, the following property holds: for all

[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\in(\mathds{R}^{p})^{2}
,

\displaystyle\left|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{n}-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}\right|

\displaystyle\leq CL^{2}\left(\frac{1+\log(1/\delta)}{n}+\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}

(128)

\displaystyle+CL^{2}\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}

(129)

\displaystyle+CL^{2}\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{S}})\cap\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)\big\|\mathbf{V}\big\|_{\Pi}

(130)

\displaystyle+\frac{CL^{2}}{n}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)\cdot\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{S}})\cap\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right).

(131)

Proof.

Let

and define the sets

\displaystyle V_{1}:=\{\mathbf{V}:\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\leq 1,\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\leq r\},\quad V_{2}:=\{\mathbf{W}:\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}\leq 1,\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})\leq\bar{r}\}.

(132)

Note that,

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(V_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\leq r\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap r^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)=:g(r),

(133)

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(V_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{r}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{S}})\cap\bar{r}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)=:\bar{g}(\bar{r}).

By Lemma

and

\displaystyle\sup_{[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\in V_{1}\times V_{2}}|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{n}-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{CL^{2}}{n}\cdot g(r)\bar{g}(\bar{r})+\frac{CL^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}[g(r)+\bar{g}(\bar{r})]

(135)

\displaystyle+CL^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\right).

(136)

We now invoke Lemma
35
with the set
V:=\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}
, functions

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle:=-\left|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{n}-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle_{\Pi}\right|,

(137)

\displaystyle h(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})

(138)

\displaystyle\bar{h}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W}),

(139)

functions
g
and
\bar{g}
as stated above and constant

. The claim follows from such lemma and the homogeneity of norms.

Proof of Proposition
2
, item (iii)

We start with the following lemma, stating a high-probability version of Chevet’s inequality. This result is suggested as an exercise in Vershynin
[
1
]
. We give a proof for completeness.

Lemma 23
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{X}
is
L
-subgaussian. Let
V
be any bounded subset of

\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}
Define
V_{1}:=\{\mathbf{V}:\exists\,\boldsymbol{u}\text{ s.t. }(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})\in V\}

and
V_{2}:=\{\boldsymbol{u}:\exists\,\mathbf{V}\text{ s.t. }(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})\in V\}
.

Then, for any
n\geq 1
and
t>0
, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-t^{2})
,

\sup_{[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in V}\langle\boldsymbol{u},\mathfrak{X}(\mathbf{V})\rangle\leq CL[\mathscr{G}\big(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(V_{1}))+\mathscr{G}\big(V_{2}\big)+t].

Proof.

For each
(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})\in V
, we define

\displaystyle Z_{\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}}

\displaystyle:=\langle\boldsymbol{u},\mathfrak{X}(\mathbf{V})\rangle=\sum_{i\in[n]}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle,\qquad W_{\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}}:=L(\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\boldsymbol{\Xi})\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\xi}\rangle),

(143)

where
\boldsymbol{\Xi}\in\mathds{R}^{p}
and
\boldsymbol{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
are independent each one having iid
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries. Therefore,

(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})\mapsto W_{\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}}
defines a centered Gaussian process indexed by
V
.

We may easily bound the
\psi_{2}
-norm of the increments using rotation invariance of sub-Gaussian random variables. Indeed, using that
\{\mathbf{X}_{i}\}
is an iid sequence and Proposition 2.6.1 in
[
1
]
, given
[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]
and
[\mathbf{V}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}]
in
V
,

\displaystyle|Z_{\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}}-Z_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}}|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\mathbf{V}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}_{i}\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\right|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}

(144)

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\mathbf{V}-\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\right|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}

(145)

\displaystyle\leq 2C\sum_{i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},(\boldsymbol{u}_{i}-\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\prime})\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\right|^{2}_{\psi_{2}}+2C\sum_{i\in[n]}\left|\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}_{i},\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\prime}(\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{V}^{\prime})\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\right|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}

(146)

\displaystyle\leq 2CL^{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}^{2}+2CL^{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\|_{\Pi}^{2}\leq 2CL^{2}\mathsf{d}([\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}],[\mathbf{V}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}]),

(147)

with the pseudo-metric
\mathsf{d}([\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}],[\mathbf{V}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}]):=\sqrt{\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\|_{\Pi}^{2}}
, using that

\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\leq 1
and
\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq 1
. On the other hand, by definition of the process
W
it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(W_{\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}}-W_{\mathbf{V}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}})^{2}]=L^{2}(\|\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\|_{\Pi}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}).

(148)

From (
147
),(
148
), we conclude that the processes
W
and
Z
satisfy the conditions of Talagrand’s majoration and minoration generic chaining bounds for sub-Gaussian processes (e.g. Theorems 8.5.5 and 8.6.1 in
[
1
]
). Hence, for any
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-t^{2}}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in V}|Z_{\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}}|\leq CL\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in V}W_{\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}}\right]+t\right\}.

(149)

In above we used that
Z_{\mathbf{V}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}=0
at
[\mathbf{V}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0}]=0
and that the diameter of
V\subset\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}^{m_{1}\times m_{2}}\times\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}
under the metric

\mathsf{d}
is less than
2\sqrt{2}
. We also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in V}W_{\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}}\bigg]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V_{1}}\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\boldsymbol{\Xi},\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbf{V})\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\bigg]+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in V_{2}}\langle\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\xi}\rangle\bigg]=\mathscr{G}\big(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(V_{1}))+\mathscr{G}(V_{2}).

(150)

Joining the two previous inequalities complete the proof of the claimed inequality.
∎

The next proposition is a restatement of item (iii) of Proposition
2

36

Proposition 5

(
\IP
)
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{X}
is
L
-subgaussian. For all

\delta\in(0,1)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, with probability at least

1-\delta
, the following property holds: for all

[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\in(\mathds{R}^{p})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{u},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{W})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq CL\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}

(151)

\displaystyle+CL\frac{\mathscr{G}\big(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}\mathbb{B}_{F}\big)}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}

(152)

\displaystyle+CL\frac{\mathscr{G}\big(\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{S}})\cap\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}\mathbb{B}_{F}\big)}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}

(153)

\displaystyle+CL\frac{\mathscr{G}\big(\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u})\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{Q}}\cap\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}\big)}{\sqrt{n}}\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}.

(154)

Proof.

Let
R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}>0
and define the sets

\displaystyle V_{1}

\displaystyle:=\{\mathbf{V}\in\mathds{R}^{p}:\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\Pi}\leq 1,\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\leq R_{1}\},

(155)

\displaystyle V_{2}

\displaystyle:=\{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq 1,\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u})\leq R_{2}\},

(156)

\displaystyle V_{3}

\displaystyle:=\{\mathbf{W}\in\mathds{R}^{p}:\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\Pi}\leq 1,\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{V})\leq R_{3}\}.

(157)

We note that

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(V_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\leq R_{1}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap R_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)=:g_{1}(R_{1}),

(158)

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}(V_{2})

\displaystyle\leq R_{2}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{Q}}\cap R_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}\right)=:g_{2}(R_{2}),

(159)

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(V_{3})\right)

\displaystyle\leq R_{3}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{S}})\cap R_{3}^{-1}\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)=:g_{3}(R_{3}).

(160)

By Lemma
23
, we have that, for any
R_{1},R_{2}>0
and

\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\sup_{[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in V_{1}\times V_{2}}\langle\boldsymbol{u},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}g_{1}(R_{1})+\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}g_{2}(R_{2})+\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log(2/\delta)}.

(161)

Next, we invoke Lemma
36
with the set
V:=\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}
, functions

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})

\displaystyle:=-\langle\boldsymbol{u},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\rangle,

(162)

\displaystyle h(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})

(163)

\displaystyle\bar{h}(\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u}),

(164)

functions
g:=g_{1}
and
\bar{g}:=g_{2}
and constant
b:=CL
. By this lemma, given
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all
[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{u},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)

(165)

\displaystyle+\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u})\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{Q}}\cap\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}\right)

(166)

\displaystyle+\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}).

(167)

Similarly, we will invoke Lemma
23
with set
V_{3}\times V_{2}
and Lemma
36
with set
V:=\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}
, functions

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u})

\displaystyle:=-\langle\boldsymbol{u},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{W})\rangle,

(168)

\displaystyle h(\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{V})

(169)

\displaystyle\bar{h}(\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u}),

(170)

functions
g:=g_{3}
and
\bar{g}:=g_{2}
and constant
b:=CL
. We obtain that, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all
[\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{u},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{W})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{S}})\cap\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)

(171)

\displaystyle+\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u})\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{Q}}\cap\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}\right)

(172)

\displaystyle+\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}).

(173)

By an union bound, we obtain that, for every
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all
[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{u},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{W})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{R}})\cap\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)+\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{S}})\cap\mathbb{B}_{F}\right)

(174)

\displaystyle+\frac{2CL}{\sqrt{n}}\mathscr{G}\left(\mathcal{Q}(\boldsymbol{u})\mathbb{B}_{\mathcal{Q}}\cap\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}\right)

(175)

\displaystyle+\frac{2CL}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}).

(176)

To finish, we use that, for any
[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]
with non-zero coordinates,

\left[\frac{\mathbf{V}}{\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}},\frac{\mathbf{W}}{\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}]\|_{\Pi}},\frac{\boldsymbol{u}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}\right]

belongs
\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{\Pi}\times\mathbb{B}_{2}^{n}
and use homogeneity of norms.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2
, item (iv)

We start by stating two auxiliary lemmas. The next result follows from a tail symmetrization-contraction argument and the Gaussian concentration inequality.

Lemma 24

(Proposition 9.2 in
[
6
]
)
.

Assume
\sigma:=|\xi|_{\psi_{2}}<\infty
. Let
U
be any bounded subset of

\mathbb{B}^{n}_{2}
. For any

,

\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in U}\langle\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{u}\rangle\leq C\sigma\left[\mathscr{G}\big(U\big)+t\right].

Next, we state the following lemma.

Lemma 25
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{X}
is
L
-subgaussian. Let

be a bounded subset of

. There exists universal constant

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{V}\in V}\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{V})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sqrt{v}+1\right)\frac{\sigma L}{\sqrt{n}}\mathscr{G}\big(\mathfrak{S}^{1/2}(V))+C\sigma L\left(\sqrt{\frac{2u}{n}}+\frac{u}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{uv}{n}}\right).

(177)

The previous lemma is immediate from Theorem

applied to the class
F:=\{\mathbf{V}\in\mathcal{B}:\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\cdot,\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\}
and Talagrand’s majorizing theorems.

\displaystyle:=(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{n}}})[1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}]+(\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}})[1+\log(1/\delta)+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}].

20
Lemmas for decomposable norms

Recall Definition
12
in Section
10
. We first remind the reader that the
\ell_{1}
and nuclear norms are decomposable.

Example 1

(
\ell_{1}
-norm)
.

Given
\mathbf{B}\in\mathds{R}^{p}
with
sparsity support

\mathscr{S}(\mathbf{B}):=\{[j,k]:\mathbf{B}_{j,k}\neq 0\}
, the
\ell_{1}
-norm in

\mathds{R}^{p}
satisfies the above decomposability condition with the map

\mathbf{V}\mapsto\mathcal{P}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{V}):=\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{B})^{c}}

where
\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{B})^{c}}
denotes the
d_{1}\times d_{2}
matrix whose entries are zero at indexes in
\mathscr{S}(\mathbf{B})
.

Example 2

(Nuclear norm)
.

Let
\mathbf{B}\in\mathds{R}^{p}
with rank
r:=\rank(\mathbf{B})
, singular values
\{\sigma_{j}\}_{j\in[r]}
and singular vector decomposition
\mathbf{B}=\sum_{j\in[r]}\sigma_{j}\boldsymbol{u}_{j}\boldsymbol{v}_{j}^{\top}
. Here
\{\boldsymbol{u}_{j}\}_{j\in[r]}
are the left singular vectors spanning the subspace
\mathcal{U}
and
\{\boldsymbol{v}_{j}\}_{j\in[r]}
are the right singular vectors spanning the subspace
\mathcal{V}
. The pair
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V})
is sometimes referred as the
low-rank support
of
\mathbf{B}
. Given subspace
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
let

\mathbf{P}_{S^{\perp}}
denote the matrix defining the orthogonal projection onto
S^{\perp}
. Then, the map

\mathbf{V}\mapsto\mathcal{P}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{V}):=\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{U}^{\perp}}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{V}^{\perp}}^{\top}

satisfy the decomposability condition for the nuclear norm
\|\cdot\|_{N}
.

In the framework of regularized least-squares regression, decomposability is mostly useful because of the following lemma.

Lemma 26

(
[
69
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a decomposable norm over
\mathds{R}^{p}
. Let
\mathbf{B},\hat{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathds{R}^{p}
and

\mathbf{V}:=\hat{\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{B}
. Then, for any
\nu\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\nu\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})+\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{B})-\mathcal{R}(\hat{\mathbf{B}})\leq(1+\nu)\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{V}))-(1-\nu)\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\perp}(\mathbf{V})).

(203)

Next, we state a well known lemma for the Slope norm that improves upon the previous lemma — when comparing it with the
\ell_{1}
-norm.

Lemma 27

(
[
6
]
)
.

Let
o\in[n]
,
\boldsymbol{\theta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|_{0}\leq o
. Set

\boldsymbol{u}:=\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}
. Then

\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|_{\sharp}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|_{\sharp}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{o}\omega_{i}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\sharp}-\sum_{i=o+1}^{n}\omega_{i}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\sharp}.

In particular, for any
\nu\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\nu\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\sharp}+\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|_{\sharp}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|_{\sharp}\leq(1+\nu)\sum_{i=1}^{o}\omega_{i}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\sharp}-(1-\nu)\sum_{i=o+1}^{n}\omega_{i}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\sharp}.

(204)

(221)

that is, (
78
). We now split our arguments in four cases.

Case 1:

\mathbf{V}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}}(2c_{0})
and
\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(2c_{0})
.

Decomposability of
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{S})
and
[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(c_{0},\gamma_{\mathcal{R}},\gamma_{\mathcal{S}},\eta)
and Cauchy-Schwarz further imply

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})+\chi\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{W})+\tau\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq(c_{0}+1)(\lambda\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{V}))+\chi\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(\mathbf{W}))+\tau\eta\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2})

(222)

\displaystyle\leq(c_{0}+1)\{\lambda^{2}R_{\mathbf{B}}^{2}+\chi^{2}R_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{2}+\tau^{2}\eta^{2}\}^{1/2}\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}.

(223)

Case 2:

\mathbf{V}\notin\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}}(2c_{0})
and

\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(2c_{0})
.

As
[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(c_{0},\gamma_{\mathcal{R}},\gamma_{\mathcal{S}},\eta)
, we get

\displaystyle c_{0}\gamma_{\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{V}))\leq c_{0}\left[\gamma_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(\mathbf{W}))+\eta\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\right].

(224)

Hence,

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})+\chi\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})+\tau\big\|\boldsymbol{u}\big\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq(c_{0}+1)(\lambda\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{V}))+\chi\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(\mathbf{W}))+\tau\eta\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2})

(225)

\displaystyle\leq 2(c_{0}+1)(\chi\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(\mathbf{W}))+\tau\eta\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2})

(226)

\displaystyle\leq 2(c_{0}+1)\{\chi^{2}R_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{2}+\tau^{2}\eta^{2}\}^{1/2}\|[\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}.

(227)

Case 3:

\mathbf{V}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}}(2c_{0})
and

\mathbf{W}\notin\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(2c_{0})
.

This case follows very similarly to Case 2, exchanging the roles between

(\mathbf{V},\mathcal{R})
and
(\mathbf{W},\mathcal{S})
. This leads to the bounds:

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})+\chi\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})+\tau\big\|\boldsymbol{u}\big\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq 2(c_{0}+1)\{\lambda^{2}R_{\mathbf{B}}^{2}+\tau^{2}\eta^{2}\}^{1/2}\|[\mathbf{V},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}.

(228)

Case 4:

\mathbf{V}\notin\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B}}(2c_{0})
and

\mathbf{W}\notin\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(2c_{0})
.

As
[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(c_{0},\gamma_{\mathcal{R}},\gamma_{\mathcal{S}},\eta)
, we get

\displaystyle c_{0}\left[\gamma_{\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{V}))+\gamma_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(\mathbf{W}))\right]\leq c_{0}\eta\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}.

(229)

Hence,

\displaystyle\lambda\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{V})+\chi\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{W})+\tau\big\|\boldsymbol{u}\big\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq(c_{0}+1)(\lambda\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{V}))+\chi\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(\mathbf{W}))+\tau\eta|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2})

(230)

\displaystyle\leq 2(c_{0}+1)\tau\eta\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}.

(231)

Relation (
79
) follows by taking the largest bounds among all four cases.

24
Proof of Proposition
3

Let

\blacksquare:=(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{4}})\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+(\nicefrac{{\chi}}{{4}})\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})+(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{4}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}
. By Lemma
17
,

\displaystyle 2\blacksquare+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(n)}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}^{2}+\left(\mathsf{d}_{1}\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}-(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sigma}})\blacktriangle\right)_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{B}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\boldsymbol{f}^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}

(232)

\displaystyle+\left(2\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{u}]\|_{\Pi}-\blacktriangle\right)+2(\blacktriangle+\blacksquare+\blacktriangledown).

(233)

By Lemmas
26
and
27
(with
\nu=1/2
) and condition (iii’),

\displaystyle\blacktriangle+\blacksquare+\blacktriangledown

\displaystyle=\left[((\sigma\mathsf{d}_{2})\vee(2\mathsf{f}_{2}))+(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{4}})\right]\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+\lambda\big(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{B})-\mathcal{R}(\hat{\mathbf{B}})\big)

(234)

\displaystyle+\left[((\sigma\mathsf{d}_{3})\vee(2\mathsf{f}_{3}+2\mathsf{f}_{*}))+(\nicefrac{{\chi}}{{4}})\right]\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})+\chi\big(\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\mathcal{S}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\big)

(235)

\displaystyle+\left[((\sigma\mathsf{d}_{4})\vee(2\mathsf{f}_{4}))+(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{4}})\right]\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}+\tau\big(\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|_{\sharp}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|_{\sharp}\big)

(236)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+\lambda\big(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{B})-\mathcal{R}(\hat{\mathbf{B}})\big)

(237)

\displaystyle+(\nicefrac{{\chi}}{{2}})\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})+\chi\big(\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\mathcal{S}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\big)

(238)

\displaystyle+(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}+\tau\big(\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|_{\sharp}-\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|_{\sharp}\big)

(239)

\displaystyle\leq\triangle_{\lambda,\chi,\tau}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}|\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}).

(240)

Next, we will define some local variables for convenience of notation. Let

G:=\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\|_{\Pi}
,

D:=\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{B}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\boldsymbol{f}^{(n)}\|_{2},

x:=\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(n)}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2},

and

r:=r_{\lambda,\chi,\tau\Omega,3}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}|\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma})
. Define also
\triangle:=\triangle_{\lambda,\chi,\tau}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}|\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma})
and

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle:=(\nicefrac{{3\lambda}}{{2}})(\mathcal{R}\circ\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}})(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+(\nicefrac{{3\chi}}{{2}})(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})+(\nicefrac{{3\tau\Omega}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2},

(241)

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle:=(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})(\mathcal{R}\circ\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\perp})(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+(\nicefrac{{\chi}}{{2}})(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\perp})(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})+(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{2}})\sum_{i=o+1}^{n}\omega_{i}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})_{i}^{\sharp}.

(242)

In particular,

\triangle=H-I.

The previous two bounds entail

\displaystyle 2\blacksquare+x^{2}+(\mathsf{d}_{1}G-(\nicefrac{{\blacktriangle}}{{\sigma}}))_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+(2\mathsf{f}_{1}G-\blacktriangle)+2\triangle.

(243)

We split our argument in two cases.

Case 1:

\mathsf{d}_{1}G\leq\blacktriangle/2\sigma
. We next show that

\triangle\leq 0
. If
\triangle>0
then

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(3,\gamma_{\mathcal{R}},\gamma_{\mathcal{S}},\Omega)
. By Lemma
18
and (iii’),

\blacktriangle\leq(\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}})[\lambda\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+\chi\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})+\tau\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\big\|_{\sharp}]\leq 2rG<2\sigma\mathsf{d}_{1}G,

where we have used condition (
61
). This is a contradiction, showing that
\triangle\leq 0
.

Now, condition (iv’) implies that
(2\mathsf{f}_{1}G-\blacktriangle)\leq\sigma(\mathsf{d}_{1}G-(\nicefrac{{\blacktriangle}}{{\sigma}}))\leq-\blacktriangle/2
. We thus obtain from (
243
) that

\displaystyle 2\blacksquare+x^{2}+\frac{\blacktriangle}{2}\leq D^{2}.

(244)

In particular,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{1}G\leq\frac{D^{2}}{\sigma}.

(245)

Case 2:

\mathsf{d}_{1}G\geq\blacktriangle/2\sigma
. In particular, from (
243
),

\displaystyle 2\blacksquare+x^{2}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{4}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+2\mathsf{f}_{1}G+2\triangle.

(246)

We next consider two cases.

Case 2.1:

\mathsf{f}_{1}G\geq H
. Hence,
\triangle\leq H\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}G
. From (
246
),

\displaystyle 2\blacksquare+x^{2}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{4}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+4\mathsf{f}_{1}G.

(247)

From
4\mathsf{f}_{1}G\leq\frac{16\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{4}
, we get

\displaystyle 2\blacksquare+x^{2}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{16\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}.

(248)

If we use instead
4\mathsf{f}_{1}G\leq\frac{32\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{8}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{8}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{32\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}.

(249)

Case 2.2:

\mathsf{f}_{1}G\leq H
. Suppose first
\triangle\leq 0
. From
2\mathsf{f}_{1}G\leq\frac{4\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{4}
and (
246
), we get

\displaystyle 2\blacksquare+x^{2}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{4\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}.

(250)

If instead we use
2\mathsf{f}_{1}G\leq\frac{8\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{8}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{8}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{8\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}.

(251)

Suppose now
\triangle\geq 0
. Hence
[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(3,\gamma_{\mathcal{R}},\gamma_{\mathcal{S}},\Omega)
. By Lemma
18
,
\triangle\leq 3rG/2
. From (
246
),

\displaystyle 2\blacksquare+x^{2}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{4}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+(2\mathsf{f}_{1}+3r)G.

(252)

Proceeding similarly before, we obtain from the displayed bound that

\displaystyle 2\blacksquare+x^{2}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{(2\mathsf{f}_{1}+3r)^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}},

(253)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{8}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{2(2\mathsf{f}_{1}+3r)^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}.

(254)

The proof of (
80
) follows by taking the largest of the bounds in (
244
), (
248
), (
250
) and (
253
). The proof of (
81
) follows by taking the largest of the bounds in (
245
), (
249
), (
251
) and (
254
).

25
Proof of Lemma
19

As
[\hat{\mathbf{B}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}]
is the minimizer of (
3
), in particular

\displaystyle[\hat{\mathbf{B}},\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]\in

\displaystyle\argmin_{[\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}]:\|\mathbf{B}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathsf{a}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{y}^{(n)}-\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{B}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{B})+\chi\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\right\}.

(255)

By the first order condition, there exist
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\in\mathds{R}^{p}
such that

\mathcal{R}^{*}(\mathbf{V})\leq 1
,
\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\hat{\mathbf{B}}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle=\mathcal{R}(\hat{\mathbf{B}})
,
\mathcal{S}^{*}(\mathbf{W})\leq 1
,
\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle=\mathcal{S}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})
, such that, for all
[\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}]
with
\|\mathbf{B}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathsf{a}
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}_{i}(\hat{\mathbf{B}}+\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})+\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}-y_{i}^{(n)}\right]\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}^{(n)}_{i},\mathbf{B}-\hat{\mathbf{B}}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle+\lambda\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{V},\mathbf{B}-\hat{\mathbf{B}}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle,

(256)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[n]}\left[\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}_{i}(\hat{\mathbf{B}}+\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})+\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}-y_{i}^{(n)}\right]\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{X}^{(n)}_{i},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle+\chi\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\mathbf{W},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle.

(257)

Summing the previous inequalities and using that

\boldsymbol{y}^{(n)}=\boldsymbol{f}^{(n)}+\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}+\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)}

we obtain, for all
[\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}]
with
\|\mathbf{B}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathsf{a}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(n)},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}),\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\right\rangle-\lambda\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle-\chi\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\mathbf{W}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle.

(258)

Moreover,

-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\mathbf{V}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\leq\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{B})-\mathcal{R}(\hat{\mathbf{B}})
. Similarly,
-\langle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\mathbf{W}\rangle\mathchoice{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-4.5mu}{{}\mkern-2.5mu}{}\rangle\leq\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\mathcal{S}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})
. Combining these bounds with the previous displays finishes the proof.

Proof of Lemma
20

By the parallelogram law,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(n)},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\rangle=

(259)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{B}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\boldsymbol{f}^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}.

(260)

By
\ATP
(with variable
\boldsymbol{u}=\mathbf{0}
),

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\mathsf{d}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]\|_{\Pi}-\mathsf{d}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})-\mathsf{d}_{3}\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\right)_{+}^{2}-2|\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\rangle|.

(261)

Also, by assumption,

|\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\rangle|\leq\mathsf{f}_{*}\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}).

The previous three displays and (
83
) imply

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}+\left(\mathsf{d}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]\|_{\Pi}-\mathsf{d}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})-\mathsf{d}_{3}\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\right)_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{B}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\boldsymbol{f}^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}

(262)

\displaystyle+2\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\rangle+2\mathsf{f}_{*}\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})

(263)

\displaystyle+2\lambda(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{B})-\mathcal{R}(\hat{\mathbf{B}}))+2\chi(\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\mathcal{S}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})).

(264)

By
\IP
,

\displaystyle\langle-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{b}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]\|_{\Pi}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}+\mathsf{b}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}+\mathsf{b}_{3}\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}

(265)

\displaystyle+\mathsf{b}_{4}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]\|_{\Pi}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}.

(266)

By
\MP
(with variable
\boldsymbol{u}=\mathbf{0}
),

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{f}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]\|_{\Pi}+\mathsf{f}_{2}\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+\mathsf{f}_{3}\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}).

(267)

The two previous displays imply

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}},\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\rangle

(268)

\displaystyle\leq\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]\|_{\Pi}\left(\mathsf{b}_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}+\mathsf{b}_{4}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}+\mathsf{f}_{1}\right)+\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})(\mathsf{b}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}+\mathsf{f}_{2})+\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})(\mathsf{b}_{3}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}+\mathsf{f}_{3}).

(269)

The proof of (
88
) follows from the previous display and (
264
).

Proof of Theorem
15

Let
\hat{\blacksquare}:=(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{4}})\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+(\nicefrac{{\chi}}{{4}})\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})
. By Lemma
20
,

\displaystyle 2\hat{\blacksquare}+\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}+\left(\mathsf{d}_{1}\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]\|_{\Pi}-(\nicefrac{{\hat{\blacktriangle}}}{{\sigma}})\right)_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{B}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\boldsymbol{f}^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}

(270)

\displaystyle+\left(2\mathsf{f}_{1}+2\mathsf{b}_{1}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}+2\mathsf{b}_{4}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}\right)\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]\|_{\Pi}-\hat{\blacktriangle}

\displaystyle+2(\hat{\blacktriangle}+\hat{\blacksquare}+\hat{\blacktriangledown}).

(272)

Additionally, all conditions of Proposition
3
hold. Hence,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{D^{2}}}{{\sigma\mathsf{d}_{1}}})\bigvee\left((\nicefrac{{2\sqrt{2}}}{{\mathsf{d}_{1}}})D+\clubsuit_{2}(\mathsf{f}_{1},r)\right)\leq\mathsf{c}_{*}\sigma,

(273)

\displaystyle(\nicefrac{{\tau}}{{2}})\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{\sharp}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\spadesuit_{2}(\mathsf{f}_{1},r)\leq\mathsf{c}_{*}^{2}\sigma^{2},

(274)

where we have used conditions (
62
)-(
63
). In particular, by (iv),

\displaystyle(\sigma\mathsf{d}_{2})\vee(2\mathsf{f}_{2}+2\mathsf{b}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda}{4},

(275)

\displaystyle(\sigma\mathsf{d}_{3})\vee(2\mathsf{f}_{3}+2\mathsf{f}_{*}+2\mathsf{b}_{3}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\chi}{4}.

(276)

By Lemma
26
(with
\nu=1/2
) and (
275
)-(
276
),

\displaystyle\hat{\blacktriangle}+\hat{\blacksquare}+\hat{\blacktriangledown}

\displaystyle=\left[((\sigma\mathsf{d}_{2})\vee(2\mathsf{f}_{2}+2\mathsf{b}_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}))+(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{4}})\right]\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+\lambda\big(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{B})-\mathcal{R}(\hat{\mathbf{B}})\big)

(277)

\displaystyle+\left[((\sigma\mathsf{d}_{3})\vee(2\mathsf{f}_{3}+2\mathsf{f}_{*}+2\mathsf{b}_{3}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\|_{2}))+(\nicefrac{{\chi}}{{4}})\right]\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})+\chi\big(\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\mathcal{S}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\big)

(278)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+\lambda\big(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{B})-\mathcal{R}(\hat{\mathbf{B}})\big)+(\nicefrac{{\chi}}{{2}})\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})+\chi\big(\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\mathcal{S}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\big)

(279)

\displaystyle\leq\triangle_{\lambda,\chi,0}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\mathbf{0}|\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma})=:\triangle.

(280)

Next, we define the local variables

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle:=(\nicefrac{{3\lambda}}{{2}})(\mathcal{R}\circ\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}})(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+(\nicefrac{{3\chi}}{{2}})(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}),

(281)

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle:=(\nicefrac{{\lambda}}{{2}})(\mathcal{R}\circ\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\perp})(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+(\nicefrac{{\chi}}{{2}})(\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}^{\perp})(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}),

(282)

noting that
\triangle=H-I
. Recall

D=\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{B}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\boldsymbol{f}^{(n)}\|_{2}.

For convenience, we define the additional local variables

G:=\|[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}]\|_{\Pi}
,

x:=\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(n)}\|_{2},

and

\hat{r}:=r_{\lambda,\chi,0,3}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}|\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma})
.
Finally, let us define the auxiliary variables

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{f}_{1}+\mathsf{b}_{1}\left((\nicefrac{{D^{2}}}{{\sigma\mathsf{d}_{1}}})\bigvee\left((\nicefrac{{2\sqrt{2}}}{{\mathsf{d}_{1}}})D+\clubsuit_{2}(\mathsf{f}_{1},r)\right)\right)+(2\mathsf{b}_{4}/\tau)\left(D^{2}+\spadesuit_{2}(\mathsf{f}_{1},r)\right),

(283)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{f}}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{f}_{1}+\mathsf{b}_{1}(\mathsf{c}_{*}\sigma)+(2\mathsf{b}_{4}/\tau)(\mathsf{c}_{*}^{2}\sigma^{2}).

(284)

Note that, from (
273
)-(
274
) and condition (v),
F\leq\hat{\mathsf{f}}_{1}\leq\sigma\mathsf{d}_{1}/2
.

From (
272
), (
273
)-(
274
),
and (
280
),

\displaystyle 2\hat{\blacksquare}+x^{2}+(\mathsf{d}_{1}G-(\nicefrac{{\hat{\blacktriangle}}}{{\sigma}}))_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+(2FG-\hat{\blacktriangle})+2\triangle.

(285)

The rest of the proof uses similar arguments used in the proof of Proposition
3
.

We split our argument in two cases.

Case 1:

\mathsf{d}_{1}G\leq\hat{\blacktriangle}/2\sigma
. We next show that

\triangle\leq 0
. If
\triangle>0
then

[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\mathbb{0}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(3,\gamma_{\mathcal{R}},\gamma_{\mathcal{S}},0)
. By Lemma
18
and (
275
)-(
276
),

\hat{\blacktriangle}\leq(\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}})[\lambda\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}})+\chi\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})]\leq 2\hat{r}G<2\sigma\mathsf{d}_{1}G,

where we have used condition (
61
). This is a contradiction, hence
\triangle\leq 0
.

From (
273
)-(
274
) and condition (v),
(2FG-\hat{\blacktriangle})\leq(2\hat{\mathsf{f}}_{1}G-\hat{\blacktriangle})\leq\sigma(\mathsf{d}_{1}\hat{G}-(\nicefrac{{\hat{\blacktriangle}}}{{\sigma}}))\leq-\hat{\blacktriangle}/2
. We thus obtain from (
285
) that

\displaystyle 2\hat{\blacksquare}+x^{2}+\frac{\hat{\blacktriangle}}{2}\leq D^{2}.

(286)

In particular,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{1}G\leq\frac{D^{2}}{\sigma}.

(287)

Case 2:

\mathsf{d}_{1}G\geq\hat{\blacktriangle}/2\sigma
. In particular, from (
285
),

\displaystyle 2\hat{\blacksquare}+x^{2}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{4}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+2FG+2\triangle.

(288)

We next consider two cases.

Case 2.1:

FG\geq H
. Hence,
\triangle\leq H\leq FG
. From (
288
),

\displaystyle 2\hat{\blacksquare}+x^{2}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{4}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+4FG.

(289)

From
4FG\leq\frac{16F^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{4}
, we get

\displaystyle 2\hat{\blacksquare}+x^{2}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{16F^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}.

(290)

If we use instead
4FG\leq\frac{32F^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{8}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{8}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{32F^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}.

(291)

Case 2.2:

FG\leq H
. Suppose first
\triangle\leq 0
. From
2FG\leq\frac{4F^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{4}
and (
288
), we get

\displaystyle 2\hat{\blacksquare}+x^{2}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{4F^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}.

(292)

If instead we use
2FG\leq\frac{8F^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{8}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{8}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{8F^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}.

(293)

Suppose now
\triangle\geq 0
. Hence
[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\mathbb{0}]\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(3,\gamma_{\mathcal{R}},\gamma_{\mathcal{S}},0)
. By Lemma
18
,
\triangle\leq 3\hat{r}G/2
. From (
288
),

\displaystyle 2\hat{\blacksquare}+x^{2}+\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{4}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+(2F+3\hat{r})G.

(294)

Proceeding similarly as before, we obtain from the displayed bound that

\displaystyle 2\hat{\blacksquare}+x^{2}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{(2F+3\hat{r})^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}},

(295)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}G^{2}}{8}

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}+\frac{2(2F+3\hat{r})^{2}}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}.

(296)

The proof of (
65
) follows by taking the largest of the bounds in (
286
), (
290
), (
292
) and (
295
). The proof of (
66
) follows by taking the largest of the bounds in (
287
), (
291
), (
293
) and (
296
).

28
Proof of Theorem
2

In the following
\mathcal{R}:=\|\cdot\|_{N}
,

\mathcal{S}:=\|\cdot\|_{1}
,
\mathsf{a}=\mathsf{a}^{*}/\sqrt{n}
and
\mathsf{f}_{*}=2\mathsf{a}^{*}/\sqrt{n}
. Recall that
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
is the identity matrix. We know that

\mathscr{G}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{B}_{\|\cdot\|_{N}})\lesssim\sqrt{d_{1}+d_{2}}
,
\mathscr{G}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{B}_{1}^{p})\lesssim\sqrt{\log p}
and

\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{B}_{\sharp})\lesssim 1
. Next, we assume that
n\geq C_{0}L^{4}(1+\log(1/\delta))
for an absolute constant to be determined next. We will also use that
L\geq 1
. In the following,
C>0
is the universal constant stated in Proposition
2
. Without loss on generality, we assume the constant
\mathsf{c}_{*}
in Theorem
15
is
\geq 1
. No effort is made to optimize the numerical constants.

By Proposition
2
(i) and taking
C_{0}\geq 1
large enough, we get that, on an event

\mathcal{E}_{1}
of probability
\geq 1-\delta
,
\PP_{\|\cdot\|_{N},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(\mathsf{c}_{1}(\delta),\mathsf{c}_{2},\mathsf{c}_{3},\mathsf{c}_{4})
holds with constants

\displaystyle\mathsf{c}_{1}(\delta)\asymp CL^{2}\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\mathsf{c}_{2}\asymp CL^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{n}},

(297)

\displaystyle\mathsf{c}_{3}\asymp CL^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}},\quad\mathsf{c}_{4}\asymp CL^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{n}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}.

(298)

By Proposition
2
(ii) and taking
C_{0}\gtrsim C^{2}
large enough, we get that, on an event
\mathcal{E}_{2}
of probability
\geq 1-2\delta
,
\TP_{\|\cdot\|_{N}}(\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2})
and

\TP_{\|\cdot\|_{1}}(\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1},\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2})
hold with constants

\mathsf{a}_{1}=\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{1}\in(0,1)
and

\displaystyle\mathsf{a}_{2}\asymp CL^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{n}},\quad\bar{\mathsf{a}}_{2}\asymp CL^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}.

(299)

By Proposition
2
(iii), on an event

\mathcal{E}_{3}
of probability
\geq 1-\delta
,
\IP_{\|\cdot\|_{N},\|\cdot\|_{1},\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}(\mathsf{b}_{1}(\delta),\mathsf{b}_{2},\mathsf{b}_{3},\mathsf{b}_{4})
holds with constants

\mathsf{b}_{4}\asymp\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{b}_{1}(\delta)\asymp CL\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\mathsf{b}_{2}\asymp CL\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{n}},\quad\mathsf{b}_{3}\asymp CL\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}.

(300)

By Proposition
2
(iv) and
C_{0}\geq 1
large enough, we have that, on an event
\mathcal{E}_{4}
of probability
\geq 1-\delta
,
\MP_{\|\cdot\|_{N},\|\cdot\|_{1},\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}(\mathsf{f}_{1}(\delta),\mathsf{f}_{2},\mathsf{f}_{3},\mathsf{f}_{4})
holds with constants
\mathsf{f}_{4}\asymp\frac{C\sigma}{\sqrt{n}}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{1}(\delta)\asymp C\sigma L\frac{2+3\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\mathsf{f}_{2}\asymp C\sigma L\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{n}},\quad\mathsf{f}_{3}\asymp C\sigma L\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}.

(301)

Finally, by Lemma
11
and taking
C_{0}\gtrsim C^{2}
large enough, we have that, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
of probability
\geq 1-5\delta
, all the previous stated properties hold and

\ATP_{\|\cdot\|_{N},\|\cdot\|_{1},\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}}(\mathsf{d}_{1},\mathsf{d}_{2},\mathsf{d}_{3},\mathsf{d}_{4})
holds with constants
\mathsf{d}_{1}\in(0,1)
,

\mathsf{d}_{4}\asymp\frac{CL}{\sqrt{n}}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{2}\asymp CL^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{n}},\quad\mathsf{d}_{3}\asymp CL^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}.

(302)

The rest of the proof will happen on the event

\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
. We will invoke the general Theorem
15
. Conditions (i)-(iii) were shown previously. We now verify conditions (iv)-(v). Note that

\displaystyle 4[(\sigma\mathsf{d}_{2})\vee(2\mathsf{f}_{2}+2\mathsf{c}_{*}\sigma\mathsf{b}_{2})]\lesssim(1+\mathsf{c}_{*})C\sigma L^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{n}}\asymp\lambda.

(303)

Also,

\displaystyle 4[(\sigma\mathsf{d}_{3})\vee(2\mathsf{f}_{3}+2\mathsf{f}_{*}+2\mathsf{c}_{*}\sigma\mathsf{b}_{3})]\lesssim(1+\mathsf{c}_{*})C\sigma L^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}+\frac{\mathsf{a}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}}\asymp\chi.

(304)

Next, we choose
\tau\asymp C_{1}\mathsf{c}_{*}^{2}C\sigma L^{2}/\sqrt{n}
for some absolute constant
C_{1}\geq 1
. We have

4[(\sigma\mathsf{d}_{4})\vee(2\mathsf{f}_{4})]\lesssim C\sigma L/\sqrt{n}\lesssim\tau.

This shows (iv). Finally, by the choice of
\tau
,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{f}}_{1}=\mathsf{f}_{1}+\mathsf{c}_{*}(\sigma\mathsf{b}_{1})+2\mathsf{c}_{*}^{2}\sigma^{2}(\nicefrac{{\mathsf{b}_{4}}}{{\tau}})\lesssim(1+\mathsf{c}_{*})C\sigma L\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{C_{1}L}\sigma,

(305)

which can be set strictly less than
\sigma\mathsf{d}_{1}/2\asymp\sigma
taking
C_{0}\gtrsim C^{2}(1+\mathsf{c}_{*})^{2}
and
C_{1}\geq 1
large enough. Hence, (v) holds.

In what follows, we verify the conditions (
58
)-(
63
). Let
[\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}]
with
D=\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{B}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\boldsymbol{f}^{(n)}\|_{2}
,
\rank(\mathbf{B})\leq r
,

\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\|_{0}\leq s
and
\|\mathbf{B}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathsf{a}^{*}/\sqrt{n}
. Conditions (
58
)-(
59
) hold. Condition (
60
) also holds since, by the dual-norm inequality, isotropy and the facts that
\|\hat{\mathbf{B}}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathsf{a}^{*}/\sqrt{n}
and
\|\mathbf{B}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathsf{a}^{*}/\sqrt{n}
,

|\langle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\rangle_{\Pi}|\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}\|_{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\|_{1}\leq\frac{2\mathsf{a}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\|_{1}.

We have that
\Psi_{\|\cdot\|_{N}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{B}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathbf{B}}))\leq\sqrt{r}
. By isotropy
\mu(\mathbf{B}):=\mu\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{B},\|\cdot\|_{N}}(6)\right)=1
. Similarly,
\Psi_{\|\cdot\|_{1}}(\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}))\leq\sqrt{s}
and

\mu(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}):=\mu\left(\mathcal{C}_{\boldsymbol{\Gamma},\|\cdot\|_{1}}(6)\right)=1
. By the choice of
(\lambda,\chi,\tau)
,

\displaystyle r^{2}

\displaystyle\asymp(1+\mathsf{c}_{*})^{2}C^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{4}\cdot\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{n}+(1+\mathsf{c}_{*})^{2}C^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{4}\cdot\frac{s\log p}{n}+\frac{(\mathsf{a}^{*})^{2}s}{n}

(306)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+C_{1}^{2}\mathsf{c}_{*}^{4}C^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{2}\epsilon\log(e/\epsilon),

(307)

where we used that
\Omega^{2}\leq 2o\log(en/o)
. We have

r<\sigma\mathsf{d}_{1}\asymp\sigma
if

\displaystyle C_{2}(1+\mathsf{c}_{*})CL^{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{n}}

\displaystyle<1,

(308)

\displaystyle C_{2}(1+\mathsf{c}_{*})CL^{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{s\log p}{n}}+\mathsf{a}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{s}{n}}

\displaystyle<1,

(309)

\displaystyle C_{1}\mathsf{c}_{*}^{2}CL^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon\log(e/\epsilon)}<c_{1},

(310)

for some absolute constants
C_{2}\geq 1
and
c_{1}\in(0,1)
.

Next, we show that
D^{2}+\spadesuit_{2}(\mathsf{f}_{1},r)\leq\mathsf{c}_{*}^{2}\sigma^{2}
. For this to be true it is sufficient that
D\leq\frac{\mathsf{c}_{*}\sigma}{3}
and

\spadesuit_{2}^{1/2}(\mathsf{f}_{1},r)\leq\frac{3\mathsf{c}_{*}\sigma}{4}
. Note that

\spadesuit_{2}^{1/2}(\mathsf{f}_{1},r)=\frac{4\mathsf{f}_{1}+3r}{\mathsf{d}_{1}}\lesssim C\sigma L\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}+r,

which is not greater than
\frac{3\mathsf{c}_{*}\sigma}{4}
if

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)CL\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\mathsf{c}_{*}}{16}

(311)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)(1+\mathsf{c}_{*})CL^{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\mathsf{c}_{*}}{16},

(312)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)(1+\mathsf{c}_{*})CL^{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{s\log p}{n}}+\mathcal{O}(1)\mathsf{a}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{s}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\mathsf{c}_{*}}{16},

(313)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)C_{1}\mathsf{c}_{*}^{2}CL^{2}\sqrt{\epsilon\log(e/\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\mathsf{c}_{*}}{16}.

(314)

Next, we show that

\left[(\nicefrac{{D^{2}}}{{\sigma\mathsf{d}_{1}}})\right]\bigvee\left[(\nicefrac{{2\sqrt{2}}}{{\mathsf{d}_{1}}})D+\clubsuit_{2}(\mathsf{f}_{1},r)\right]\leq\mathsf{c}_{*}\sigma.

For that to happen it is sufficient that

D\leq[(\sqrt{\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{c}_{*}})\wedge(\mathsf{d}_{1}\mathsf{c}_{*}/6\sqrt{2})]\sigma

and
\clubsuit_{2}(\mathsf{f}_{1},r)\leq\frac{3\mathsf{c}_{*}\sigma}{4}
. Since
\mathsf{d}_{1}\asymp 1
and
\mathsf{c}_{*}\geq 1
, the first relation is guaranteed if
D\lesssim\sqrt{\mathsf{c}_{*}}\sigma
. We have

\clubsuit_{2}(\mathsf{f}_{1},r)=\frac{16\mathsf{f}_{1}+12r}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}\lesssim C\sigma L\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}+r,

which is not greater than
\frac{3\mathsf{c}_{*}\sigma}{4}
if all the four conditions in the previous display are met — up to enlarging the constants if necessary.

Optimizing the previous inequalities, we conclude that for conditions (
58
)-(
63
) to hold it is sufficient to take
\mathsf{c}_{*}\asymp 1
large enough,
C_{0}\gtrsim C^{2}
large enough and that (
308
), (
309
), (
310
) hold (possibly enlarging
C_{2}
if necessary) and
D\lesssim\sigma
holds (possibly decreasing the constant if necessary).

In conclusion, assume
n\geq C_{0}L^{4}(1+\log(1/\delta))
with
C_{0}\gtrsim C^{2}
large enough and set the hyper-parameters to be

\displaystyle\lambda\asymp C\sigma L^{2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{n}},\quad\chi\asymp C\sigma L^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}+\frac{\mathsf{a}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\tau\asymp\frac{C_{1}C\sigma L^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(315)

Assume (
308
), (
309
), (
310
) hold. Let
[\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}]
with
D=\|\mathfrak{X}^{(n)}(\mathbf{B}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\boldsymbol{f}^{(n)}\|_{2}\lesssim\sigma
,

\rank(\mathbf{B})\leq r
,
\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\|_{0}\leq s
and

\|\mathbf{B}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathsf{a}^{*}/\sqrt{n}
. Then conditions (i)-(v) and conditions (
58
)-(
63
) of Theorem
15
are satisfied, implying (
65
)-(
66
) for such
[\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}]
. We now verify the statement of Theorem
2
.

First, note that

\displaystyle\hat{r}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{4}\cdot\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{n}+C^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{4}\cdot\frac{s\log p}{n}+\frac{(\mathsf{a}^{*})^{2}s}{n}

(316)

\displaystyle r^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{r}^{2}+C_{1}^{2}C^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{4}\epsilon\log(e/\epsilon),

(317)

Next, using that
D\lesssim\sigma
,
\mathsf{d}_{1}\asymp 1
,
n\geq C_{0}L^{4}(1+\log(1/\delta))
and
\mathsf{b}_{1}r\leq\frac{\sigma\mathsf{b}_{1}^{2}}{2}+\frac{r^{2}}{2\sigma}
,

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathsf{f}_{1}+\mathsf{b}_{1}(\sigma+\mathsf{f}_{1}+r)+\frac{1}{C_{1}L\sigma}(D^{2}+(\mathsf{f}_{1}+r)^{2})

(319)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\mathsf{b}_{1})\mathsf{f}_{1}+\sigma\mathsf{b}_{1}+\frac{\sigma\mathsf{b}_{1}^{2}}{2}+\frac{r^{2}}{2\sigma}+\frac{D}{C_{1}L}+\frac{\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}}{C_{1}L\sigma}+\frac{r^{2}}{C_{1}L\sigma}

(320)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{D}{C_{1}L}+\mathsf{f}_{1}+\sigma\mathsf{b}_{1}+\frac{r^{2}}{C_{1}L\sigma}.

(321)

From (
308
)-(
310
), we can write

\displaystyle\frac{r^{4}}{C_{1}^{2}L^{2}\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\nicefrac{{C}}{{C_{1}}})^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{2}\cdot\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{n}+(\nicefrac{{C}}{{C_{1}}})^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{2}\cdot\frac{s\log p}{n}+\frac{1}{C_{1}^{2}L^{2}\sigma^{2}}\cdot\frac{(\mathsf{a}^{*})^{2}s}{n}

(322)

\displaystyle+C_{1}^{2}C^{4}\sigma^{2}L^{6}\epsilon^{2}\log^{2}(e/\epsilon).

(323)

We have that

\displaystyle\spadesuit_{2}(F,\hat{r})=\frac{1}{\mathsf{d}_{1}^{2}}(4F+3\hat{r})^{2}\ \lesssim F^{2}+\hat{r}^{2}\lesssim\frac{D^{2}}{C_{1}^{2}L^{2}}+\mathsf{f}_{1}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\mathsf{b}_{1}^{2}+\hat{r}^{2}+\frac{r^{4}}{C_{1}^{2}L^{2}\sigma^{2}}.

(324)

It follows that

\displaystyle D^{2}+\spadesuit_{2}(F,\hat{r})

\displaystyle\leq D^{2}\left(1+\frac{\mathcal{O}(1)}{C_{1}^{2}L^{2}}\right)

(325)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)C^{2}\cdot\sigma^{2}L^{2}\frac{1+\log(1/\delta)}{n}

(326)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)C^{2}\cdot\sigma^{2}L^{4}\cdot\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{n}+\mathcal{O}(1)C^{2}\cdot\sigma^{2}L^{4}\cdot\frac{s\log p}{n}+\left(1+\frac{\mathcal{O}(1)}{C_{1}^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{2}}\right)\frac{(\mathsf{a}^{*})^{2}s}{n}

(327)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)C^{4}\cdot C_{1}^{2}\sigma^{2}L^{6}\epsilon^{2}\log^{2}(e/\epsilon).

(328)

This establishes (
19
) in Theorem
2
using (
65
) in Theorem
15
.

Note that, since
D\lesssim\sigma
and

\mathsf{d}_{1}\asymp 1
,

(\nicefrac{{D^{2}}}{{\sigma\mathsf{d}_{1}}})\bigvee\left[(\nicefrac{{2\sqrt{2}}}{{\mathsf{d}_{1}}})D+\clubsuit_{2}(F,\hat{r})\right]\lesssim D+\clubsuit_{2}(F,\hat{r}).

Also,
\clubsuit_{2}(F,\hat{r})\lesssim\spadesuit_{2}^{1/2}(F,\hat{r})
. Thus

\displaystyle(\nicefrac{{D^{2}}}{{\sigma\mathsf{d}_{1}}})\bigvee\left[(\nicefrac{{2\sqrt{2}}}{{\mathsf{d}_{1}}})D+\clubsuit_{2}(F,\hat{r})\right]

\displaystyle\leq D\left(\mathcal{O}(1)+\frac{\mathcal{O}(1)}{C_{1}L}\right)

(329)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)C\cdot\sigma L\frac{1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}

(330)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)C\cdot\sigma L^{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{n}}

(331)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)C\cdot\sigma L^{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{s\log p}{n}}+\left(1+\frac{\mathcal{O}(1)}{C_{1}\sigma L}\right)\mathsf{a}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{s}{n}}

(332)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(1)C^{2}\cdot C_{1}\sigma L^{3}\epsilon\log(e/\epsilon).

(333)

This establishes (
20
) in Theorem
2
using (
66
) in Theorem
15
.

29
Proof of Proposition
1

In trace-regression with matrix decomposition, the design is random. Up to conditioning on the feature data, the proof of Proposition
1
follows almost identical arguments in the proof of Theorem 2 in
[
2
]
for the matrix decomposition problem with identity design
(\mathfrak{X}=I)
. We present a sketch here for completeness.

We prepare the ground so to apply Fano’s method. For any

\boldsymbol{\Theta}:=[\mathbf{B},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}]\in(\mathds{R}^{p})^{2}
, we define for convenience

\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}\|_{F}^{2}:=\|\mathbf{B}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\|_{F}^{2}.

Given
\eta>0
and
M\in\mathbb{N}
, a
\eta
-packing of
\mathcal{A}(r,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
of size
M
is a finite subset

\mathcal{A}=\{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{M}\}
of

\mathcal{A}(r,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
satisfying

\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\ell}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}\|_{F}\geq\eta

for all
\ell\neq k
. For model (
4
), let
y_{1}^{n}:=\{y_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
and
\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}:=\{\mathbf{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
.
For any
k\in[M]
and
i\in[n]
, we will denote by

P^{k}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}

(or
P^{k}_{y_{i}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}
)
the conditional distribution of
y_{1}^{n}
(or
y_{i}
) given

\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}
corresponding to the model (
4
) with parameters
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}=[\mathbf{B}_{k},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}]
belonging to the packing
\mathcal{A}
. Being normal distributions, they are mutually absolute continuous. We denote by
\mathsf{KL}(\mathbf{P}\|\mathbf{Q})
the Kullback-Leibler divergence between probability measures
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{Q}
. In that setting, Fano’s method assures that

\displaystyle\inf_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}\in\mathcal{A}(r,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})}\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}}\left\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}\geq\eta^{2}\right\}\geq 1-\frac{\frac{1}{\binom{M}{2}}\sum_{k,\ell=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\mathsf{KL}\left(P^{k}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\big\|P^{\ell}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\right)+\log 2}{\log M}.

(334)

The proof follows from an union bound on the following two separate lower bounds.

Lower bound on the low-spikeness bias
. It is sufficient to give a lower bound on
\mathcal{A}(1,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
. We define the subset
\mathcal{A}
of
\mathcal{A}(1,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
with size
M=4
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}:=\{[\mathbf{B}^{*},-\mathbf{B}^{*}],[-\mathbf{B}^{*},\mathbf{B}^{*}],(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})[\mathbf{B}^{*},-\mathbf{B}^{*}],[\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}]\}

(335)

using the matrix
\mathbf{B}^{*}\in\mathds{R}^{p}
defined by

\mathbf{B}^{*}:=\frac{\mathsf{a}^{*}}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{array}\right]\underbrace{\left[\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}1&1&\cdots&1&0&\cdots&0\end{array}\right]^{\top}}_{\boldsymbol{f}^{\top}},

where
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
has
s
unit coordinates. It is easy to check that
\mathcal{A}
is a
\eta
-packing of

\mathcal{A}(1,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
with
\eta=c_{0}\mathsf{a}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{s}{n}}
for some constant
c_{0}>0
. For any element
[\mathbf{B}_{k},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}]
of
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathbf{B}_{k}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{k}=0
implying
P^{k}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{n}(0,n\sigma^{2}\mathbf{I})
. Hence, for any
k\neq\ell
,

\mathsf{KL}\left(P^{k}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\big\|P^{\ell}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\right)=0.

From (
334
), one obtains a lower bound with rate of order
\mathsf{a}^{*}\sqrt{\frac{s}{n}}
with positive probability.

Lower bound on the estimation error
. From the packing constructions in Lemmas 5 and 6 in
[
2
]
, one may show that, for
d_{1},d_{2}\geq 10
,
\mathsf{a}^{*}\geq 32\sqrt{\log p}
,
s<p
and any
\eta>0
, there exists a
\eta
-packing
\mathcal{A}=\{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}\}_{k\in[M]}
of
\mathcal{A}(r,s,\mathsf{a}^{*})
with size

\displaystyle M\geq\frac{1}{4}\exp\left\{\frac{s}{2}\log\frac{p-s}{s/2}+\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{256}\right\},

(336)

satisfying
\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}\|_{F}\leq 3\eta
for any
k\in[M]
. By independence of
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
and
\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}
and isotropy,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\mathsf{KL}\left(P^{k}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\big\|P^{\ell}_{y_{1}^{n}|\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\right)=\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{X}_{1}^{n}}\mathsf{KL}\left(P^{k}_{y_{i}|\mathbf{X}_{i}}\big\|P^{\ell}_{y_{i}|\mathbf{X}_{i}}\right)=\frac{n\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{k}\|_{F}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\leq\frac{18n}{\sigma^{2}}\eta^{2}.

(337)

From (
334
) and (
336
), one then checks that tacking

\displaystyle\eta^{2}:=c_{o}\sigma^{2}\left\{\frac{r(d_{1}+d_{2})}{n}+\frac{s}{n}\log\left(\frac{p-s}{s/2}\right)\right\},

(338)

for some constant
c_{0}>0
, one obtains a lower bound with rate of order
\eta^{2}
with positive probability.
