Appendix A
Peeling lemmas

This section presents several peeling lemmas. This is a well known technique in Empirical Process theory in order to lift confidence statements from a compact subset to the entire set. Throughout this section,
g,\bar{g}
are right-continuous, non-decreasing functions from
\mathbb{R}_{+}
to
\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
V
is an arbitrary set and
h,\bar{h}
are functions from
V
to
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. We let
g^{-1}
be the generalized inverse of
g
defined by
g^{-1}(x)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb{R}_{+}:g(a)\geq x\}
; we use the same notation for the generalized inverse of
\bar{g}
.
\mathbb{N}^{*}
is the set of natural numbers excluding zero. In what follows,
C>0
is an universal constant that may change within the text.

A.1
Peeling for
\PP

Lemma 35
.

Let
b>0
be a constant and
c\geq 1
be an universal constant. Assume that, for every
r,\bar{r}>0
and every
\delta\in(0,1/c]
, the event
A(r,\bar{r},\delta)
defined by the inequality

\displaystyle\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in V:(h,\bar{h})(\boldsymbol{v})\leq(r,\bar{r})}M(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{b}{n}g(r)\bar{g}(\bar{r})-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}(g(r)+\bar{g}(\bar{r}))-b\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\right),

(524)

has probability at least
1-c\delta
.

Then, for every
\delta\in(0,1/c]
, with probability at least
1-c\delta
, it holds that, for all

\boldsymbol{v}\in V
,

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\geq-C\frac{b}{n}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\bar{g}\circ\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})-Cb\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})-Cb\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\bar{g}\circ\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})

(525)

\displaystyle-Cb\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{n}\right)-Cb\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}\right).

(526)

Proof.

Let
\mu>0
and
\eta,\epsilon>1
be parameters to be chosen later on. We set
\mu_{0}=0
and, for
k\geq 1
,
\mu_{k}:=\mu\eta^{k-1}
. We may partition the set
V
with the sets

V_{k,\bar{k}}:=\{\boldsymbol{v}\in V:\mu_{k}\leq(g\circ h)(\boldsymbol{v})<\mu_{k+1},\mu_{\bar{k}}\leq(\bar{g}\circ\bar{h})(\boldsymbol{v})<\mu_{\bar{k}+1}\},

defined for
k,\bar{k}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we set

\nu_{m}:=g^{-1}(\mu_{m})
and
\bar{\nu}_{m}:=\bar{g}^{-1}(\mu_{m})
. Clearly,
g(\nu_{m})=\mu_{m}
and

\bar{g}(\bar{\nu}_{m})=\mu_{m}
.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1/c]
. An union bound and the fact that
\sum_{k\geq 1,\bar{k}\geq 1}(k\bar{k})^{-1-\epsilon}\leq(1+\epsilon^{-1})^{2}
imply that the event

A:=\bigcap_{k\geq 1,\bar{k}\geq 1}^{\infty}A\left(\nu_{k},\bar{\nu}_{\bar{k}},\frac{\epsilon^{2}\delta}{(1+\epsilon)^{2}(k\bar{k})^{1+\epsilon}}\right),

has a probability at least
1-c\delta
.

To ease notation, define

\triangle(\delta):=\log\{(1+\epsilon)^{2}/(\epsilon^{2}\delta)\}
and
\triangle_{k,\bar{k}}:=({1+\epsilon})\log(k\bar{k})
. We assume in the sequel that the event
A
is realized, that is,

\displaystyle\forall k,\bar{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\quad\begin{cases}\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in V\text{ such that }(h,\bar{h})(\boldsymbol{v})\leq(\nu_{k},\bar{\nu}_{\bar{k}})\text{ we have }\\
M(\boldsymbol{v})\geq-\frac{b}{n}g(\nu_{k})\bar{g}(\bar{\nu}_{k})-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}g(\nu_{k})-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\bar{g}(\bar{\nu}_{\bar{k}})\\
-(\nicefrac{{b}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{k,\bar{k}}}-(\nicefrac{{b}}{{n}})[\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{k,\bar{k}}].\end{cases}

(527)

For every
\boldsymbol{v}\in V
, there are
\ell,\bar{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\boldsymbol{v}\in V_{\ell,\bar{\ell}}
. We now consider several cases.

Case 1:

\ell=\bar{\ell}=0
. Since
h(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\nu_{1}
,
\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\bar{\nu}_{1}
, (
527
) with
k=\bar{k}=1
leads to

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})\geq-\frac{b}{n}\mu^{2}-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\mu-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\mu-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}-\frac{b}{n}\triangle(\delta).

(528)

Case 2:

\ell\geq 1
and
\bar{\ell}\geq 1
. Using that
h(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\nu_{\ell+1}
and
\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\bar{\nu}_{\bar{\ell}+1}
, we will invoke (
527
) for the indexes
(k,\bar{k})=(\ell+1,\bar{\ell}+1)
. Additionally, we observe that, since

\boldsymbol{v}\in V_{\ell,\bar{\ell}}
, and

\mu\eta^{\ell+1}=\eta^{2}\mu_{\ell}
,

\displaystyle g(\nu_{\ell+1})=\eta\mu^{\ell}=\eta\mu^{\ell+1}+\eta\mu^{\ell}-\eta\mu^{\ell+1}\leq\eta^{2}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})+\mu\eta^{\ell}-\mu\eta^{\ell+1}.

(529)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\bar{g}(\nu_{\bar{\ell}+1})

\displaystyle\leq\eta^{2}\bar{g}\circ\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})+\mu\eta^{\bar{\ell}}-\mu\eta^{\bar{\ell}+1},

(530)

\displaystyle g(\nu_{\ell+1})\cdot\bar{g}(\nu_{\bar{\ell}+1})

\displaystyle\leq\eta^{4}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\bar{g}\circ\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})+\mu^{2}\eta^{\ell}\eta^{\bar{\ell}}-\mu^{2}\eta^{\ell+1}\eta^{\bar{\ell}+1}.

(531)

Next, we will use the previous bounds in (
527
). To ease notation, we define the quantity

\displaystyle\lozenge_{\ell,\bar{\ell}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{b}{n}\mu^{2}(\eta^{\ell}\eta^{\bar{\ell}}-\eta^{\ell+1}\eta^{\bar{\ell}+1})+\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\mu(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})+\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\mu(\eta^{\bar{\ell}}-\eta^{\bar{\ell}+1})

(532)

\displaystyle+\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{\ell+1,\bar{\ell}+1}}-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}

(533)

\displaystyle+\frac{b}{n}[\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{\ell+1,\bar{\ell}+1}]-\frac{b}{n}\triangle(\delta).

(534)

In conclusion, from (
527
) with

(k,\bar{k})=(\ell+1,\bar{\ell}+1)
and the bounds (
529
), (
530
) and (
531
), we may write

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})+\lozenge_{\ell,\bar{\ell}}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{b}{n}\eta^{4}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\bar{g}\circ\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\eta^{2}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\eta^{2}\bar{g}\circ\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})

(535)

\displaystyle-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}-\frac{b}{n}\triangle(\delta).

(536)

To finish, we claim that, by appropriately fixing
\mu>0
and

\epsilon,\eta>1
, we can show

\sup_{\ell,\bar{\ell}\geq 1}\lozenge_{\ell,\bar{\ell}}\leq 0.

We prove this claim next.

First,

\displaystyle T_{1}(\ell,\bar{\ell})

\displaystyle:=\frac{b}{n}\triangle_{\ell+1,\bar{\ell}+1}+\frac{b\mu^{2}}{n}(\eta^{\ell}\eta^{\bar{\ell}}-\eta^{\ell+1}\eta^{\bar{\ell}+1})

(537)

\displaystyle=\frac{b}{n}\eta^{\ell}\eta^{\bar{\ell}}\left[(1+\epsilon)\frac{\log(\ell+1)+\log(\bar{\ell}+1)}{\eta^{\ell}\eta^{\bar{\ell}}}+\mu^{2}(1-\eta^{2})\right].

(538)

Since, for
\eta>1
,

\sup_{\ell,\bar{\ell}\geq 1}\frac{\log(\ell+1)+\log(\bar{\ell}+1)}{\eta^{\ell}\eta^{\bar{\ell}}}\leq C,

we can fix
\epsilon>1
and
\mu>0
and take
\eta>1
large enough such that

\sup_{\ell,\bar{\ell}\geq 1}T_{1}(\ell,\bar{\ell})\leq 0
.

Second, we note that

\displaystyle\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{\ell+1,\bar{\ell}+1}}-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle_{\ell+1,\bar{\ell}+1}}.

(539)

Hence,

\displaystyle T_{2}(\ell,\bar{\ell})

\displaystyle:=\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\mu(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})+\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\mu(\eta^{\bar{\ell}}-\eta^{\bar{\ell}+1})

(540)

\displaystyle+\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{\ell+1,\bar{\ell}+1}}-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}

(541)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\sqrt{\log(\ell+1)}+\mu(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})\right]

(542)

\displaystyle+\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\sqrt{\log(\bar{\ell}+1)}+\mu(\eta^{\bar{\ell}}-\eta^{\bar{\ell}+1})\right]

(543)

\displaystyle=\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\eta^{\ell}\left[\frac{\sqrt{\log(\ell+1)}}{\eta^{\ell}}+\mu(1-\eta)\right]

(544)

\displaystyle+\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\eta^{\ell}\left[\frac{\sqrt{\log(\ell+1)}}{\eta^{\ell}}+\mu(1-\eta)\right].

(545)

Since, for
\eta>1
,

\sup_{\ell\geq 1}\frac{\sqrt{\log(\ell+1)}}{\eta^{\ell}}\leq C,

again, we may fix
\epsilon>1
and
\mu>0
and take
\eta>1
large enough such that

\sup_{\ell,\bar{\ell}\geq 1}T_{2}(\ell,\bar{\ell})\leq 0
.

We thus conclude that

\sup_{\ell,\bar{\ell}\geq 1}\lozenge_{\ell,\bar{\ell}}\leq\sup_{\ell,\bar{\ell}\geq 1}[T_{1}(\ell,\bar{\ell})+T_{2}(\ell,\bar{\ell})]\leq 0,

as claimed.

Case 3:

\ell\geq 1
and
\bar{\ell}=0
.
Since
h(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\nu_{\ell+1}
and
\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\bar{\nu}_{1}
, we get from (
527
) with
(k,\bar{k})=(\ell+1,1)
and (
529
),

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})+\square_{\ell}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{b}{n}\mu\eta^{2}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\eta^{2}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\mu-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}-\frac{b}{n}\triangle(\delta),

(546)

where have defined

\displaystyle\square_{\ell}

\displaystyle:=\frac{b}{n}\mu^{2}(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})+\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\mu(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})

(547)

\displaystyle+\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{\ell+1,1}}-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}

(548)

\displaystyle+\frac{b}{n}[\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{\ell+1,1}]-\frac{b}{n}\triangle(\delta).

(549)

We claim that, by appropriately fixing
\mu>0
and

\epsilon>1
and taking
\eta>1
large enough, we can show

\sup_{\ell\geq 1}\square_{\ell}\leq 0.

Indeed, the reasoning is analogous to the one in Case 2 so we omit it.

Case 4:

\ell=0
and
\bar{\ell}\geq 1
.
By analogy with Case 3, using that
h(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\nu_{1}
and
\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\nu_{\bar{\ell}+1}
, we get from (
527
) with
(k,\bar{k})=(1,\bar{\ell}+1)
and (
530
),

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})+\bigcirc_{\bar{\ell}}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{b}{n}\mu\eta^{2}\bar{g}\circ\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\mu-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\eta^{2}\bar{g}\circ\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}-\frac{b}{n}\triangle(\delta),

(550)

where the term
\bigcirc_{\bar{\ell}}
can be shown to satisfy

\sup_{\bar{\ell}\geq 1}\bigcirc_{\bar{\ell}}\leq 0,

for fixed
\mu>0
and
\epsilon>1
and large enough
\eta>1
.

The proof of the statement of the lemma is finished once we join the lower bounds established in the four cases.
∎

A.2
Peeling for
\IP

The following lemma is a restatement of Lemma 6 in
[
27
]
. The proof follows by similar arguments used in the proof of Lemma
35
.

Lemma 36
.

Let
b>0
be a constant and
c\geq 1
be an universal constant. Assume that, for every
r,\bar{r}>0
and every
\delta\in(0,1/c]
, the event
A(r,\bar{r},\delta)
defined by the inequality

\displaystyle\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in V:(h,\bar{h})(\boldsymbol{v})\leq(r,\bar{r})}M(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}g(r)-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\bar{g}(\bar{r})-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log(2/\delta)},

(551)

has probability at least
1-c\delta
.

Then, for every
\delta\in(0,1/c]
, with probability at least
1-c\delta
, it holds that, for all

\boldsymbol{v}\in V
,

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{Cb}{\sqrt{n}}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})-\frac{Cb}{\sqrt{n}}\bar{g}\circ\bar{h}(\boldsymbol{v})-\frac{Cb}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}).

(552)

A.3
Peeling for
\MP

To prove Proposition
6
, we will use Lemma
36
(with
\bar{g}=\bar{h}\equiv 0
) and the following lemma.

Lemma 37
.

Let
b>0
be a constant and
c\geq 1
be universal constants. Assume that for every
r>0
and every
\delta\in(0,1/c]
, the event
A(r,\delta)
defined by the inequality

\displaystyle\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in V:h(\boldsymbol{v})\leq r}M(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\geq-\left[1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}\right]b\frac{g(r)}{\sqrt{n}}

(553)

\displaystyle-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}-\frac{b}{n}\log(1/\delta),

(554)

has probability at least
1-c\delta
.

Then, with probability at least
1-c\delta
, we have that, for all
\boldsymbol{v}\in V
,

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\geq-C\left\{[1+(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}]\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{b}{n}\right\}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})

(555)

\displaystyle-C(\nicefrac{{b}}{{\sqrt{n}}})[1+\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}]-C(\nicefrac{{b}}{{n}})[\triangle(\delta)+\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}].

(556)

Proof.

Let
\mu>0
and
\eta,\epsilon>1
be two parameters to be chosen later on. We set
\mu_{0}=0
and, for
k\geq 1
,
\mu_{k}:=\mu\eta^{k-1}
. Define, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the sets

V_{k}:=\{\boldsymbol{v}\in V:\mu_{k}\leq(g\circ h)(\boldsymbol{v})<\mu_{k+1}\}.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we set
\nu_{k}:=g^{-1}(\mu_{k})
.

An union bound and the fact that
\sum_{k\geq 1}k^{-1-\epsilon}\leq 1+\epsilon^{-1}
imply that the event

A:=\bigcap_{k=1}^{\infty}A\left(\nu_{k},\frac{\epsilon\delta}{(1+\epsilon)k^{1+\epsilon}}\right),

has a probability at least
1-c\delta
. For convenience, we define
\triangle(t):=\log\{(1+\epsilon)/(\epsilon t)\}
and
\triangle_{k}:=({1+\epsilon})\log k
. Throughout the proof, assume that this event is realized:

\displaystyle\forall k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\quad\begin{cases}\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in V\text{ such that }h(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\nu_{k}\text{ we have }\\
M(\boldsymbol{v})\geq-[1+(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{k}}]b\frac{g(\nu_{k})}{\sqrt{n}}-(\nicefrac{{b}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{k}}-(\nicefrac{{b}}{{n}})[\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{k}].\end{cases}

(557)

For every
\boldsymbol{v}\in V
, there is

\ell\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\boldsymbol{v}\in V_{\ell}
.

Case 1:

\ell=0
. In that case, (
557
) with
k=1
and using
g(\nu_{1})=\mu
lead to

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle\geq-[1+(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}](\nicefrac{{b\mu}}{{\sqrt{n}}})-(\nicefrac{{b}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}-(\nicefrac{{b}}{{n}})\triangle(\delta).

(558)

Case 2:

\ell\geq 1
. Since
v\in V_{\ell}
,
h(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\nu_{\ell+1}
, so we can invoke (
557
) for
k=\ell+1
.

First, we make some observations. Recall that
g(\nu_{\ell+1})=\mu\eta^{\ell}
. Since
\eta>1
, there is universal constant
C\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\triangle_{\ell+1}}\mu\eta^{\ell}\leq C\sqrt{(1+\epsilon)}\mu\eta^{\ell+1}\leq C\sqrt{(1+\epsilon)}\eta^{2}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v}).

(559)

Additionally,

\displaystyle\mu\eta^{\ell}=\mu\eta^{\ell+1}+\mu(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})\leq\eta^{2}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})+\mu(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1}).

(560)

We thus conclude that

\displaystyle[1+(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{\ell+1}}]b\frac{g(\nu_{\ell+1})}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\leq[1+(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}]\frac{b\mu\eta^{\ell}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\triangle_{\ell+1}}\frac{b\mu\eta^{\ell}}{n}

(561)

\displaystyle\leq[1+(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}]\frac{b\eta^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})

(562)

\displaystyle+[1+(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}]\frac{b\mu}{\sqrt{n}}(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})

(563)

\displaystyle+C\sqrt{(1+\epsilon)}\frac{\eta^{2}b}{n}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v}).

(564)

In order to use the previous bound in (
557
), it will be convenient to define the quantity:

\displaystyle\lozenge_{\ell}

\displaystyle:=[1+(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}]\frac{b\mu}{\sqrt{n}}(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})

(565)

\displaystyle+(\nicefrac{{b}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{\ell+1}}-(\nicefrac{{b}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}

(566)

\displaystyle+(\nicefrac{{b}}{{n}})[\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{\ell+1}]-(\nicefrac{{b}}{{n}})\triangle(\delta).

(567)

In conclusion, from (
557
) (with
k=\ell+1
) and (
564
), we obtain

\displaystyle M(\boldsymbol{v})+\lozenge_{\ell}

\displaystyle\geq-\left\{[1+(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}]\frac{b\eta^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}+C\sqrt{(1+\epsilon)}\frac{\eta^{2}b}{n}\right\}g\circ h(\boldsymbol{v})

(568)

\displaystyle-(\nicefrac{{b}}{{\sqrt{n}}})\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}-(\nicefrac{{b}}{{n}})\triangle(\delta).

(569)

Next, we claim that, by appropriately fixing
\mu>0
and

\epsilon,\eta>1
, we can show

\displaystyle\sup_{\ell\geq 1}\lozenge_{\ell}\leq 0.

(570)

We present the proof of the claim next.

First, since
\triangle(\delta)\geq 1
,

\displaystyle T_{1}(\ell)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}\frac{b\mu}{n}(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})+\frac{b}{n}\triangle_{\ell+1}

(571)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}\frac{b}{n}\left[\mu(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})+\log(\ell+1)\right]

(572)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}\frac{b}{n}\eta^{\ell}\left[\mu(1-\eta)+\frac{\log(\ell+1)}{\eta^{\ell}}\right].

(573)

Since, for
\eta>1
,

\sup_{\ell\geq 1}\frac{\log(\ell+1)}{\eta^{\ell}}\leq C,

we may fix
\epsilon>1
and
\mu>0
and take
\eta>1
large enough such that

\sup_{\ell\geq 1}T_{1}(\ell)\leq 0
.

Secondly,

\displaystyle T_{2}(\ell)

\displaystyle:=\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\mu(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})+\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)+\triangle_{\ell+1}}-\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\triangle(\delta)}

(574)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\sqrt{\log(\ell+1)}+\mu(\eta^{\ell}-\eta^{\ell+1})\right]

(575)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{\sqrt{n}}\eta^{\ell}\left[\frac{\sqrt{\log(\ell+1)}}{\eta^{\ell}}+\mu(1-\eta)\right].

(576)

Again, since, for
\eta>1
,

\sup_{\ell\geq 1}\frac{\sqrt{\log(\ell+1)}}{\eta^{\ell}}\leq C,

we may fix
\epsilon>1
and
\mu>0
and take
\eta>1
large enough such that

\sup_{\ell\geq 1}T_{2}(\ell)\leq 0
.

In conclusion, since
\lozenge_{\ell}=T_{1}(\ell)+T_{2}(\ell)
, we have proved (
570
).

Joining the lower bounds from both cases, we prove the statement of the lemma.
∎

Appendix B
The Multiplier Process

In this section we prove Theorem
6
. We refer to the definitions and notations in Section
7
. Given
f,g\in L_{\psi_{2}}
, we set

\langle f,g\rangle_{n}:=\hat{\mathbf{P}}fg

and

\|f\|_{n}:=\sqrt{\langle f,f\rangle_{n}}.

We recall the Hölder-type inequality
\|fg\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\|f\|_{\psi_{2}}\|g\|_{\psi_{2}}
.

Our proof is inspired by Dirksen’s method
[
37
]
which obtained concentration inequalities for the
quadratic process
. One key observation used by Dirksen
[
37
]
and Bednorz
[
5
]
is that one must bound the chain differently for
k\leq\lfloor\log_{2}n\rfloor
, the so called “subgaussian path” and
k\geq\lfloor\log_{2}n\rfloor
, the “subexponential path”. In bounding the multiplier process, we additionally introduce a “lazy walked” chain, a technique already present in Talagrand’s original bound for the
empirical process

[
83
]
.

We first present some preliminary lemmas.

Lemma 38
.

Let
f,f^{\prime}\in L_{\psi_{2}}
.

If for
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
2^{k/2}\leq\sqrt{n}
, then for any
u\geq 1
, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-(2^{k}+u))
,

\displaystyle|M(f)-M(f^{\prime})|\leq\left[(1+\sqrt{2})\frac{2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{2u}{n}}+\frac{u}{n}\right]\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{\psi_{2}}.

(577)

If for
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
2^{k/2}\geq\sqrt{n}
, then for any
u\geq 1
, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-(2^{k}+u))
,

\displaystyle\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{n}\leq(\sqrt{u}+2^{k/2})\frac{[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{d}(f,f^{\prime}).

(578)

Proof.

Suppose
2^{\frac{k}{2}}\leq\sqrt{n}
. We first note that, by the triangle and Hölder-type inequalities for the norm
\psi_{1}
, we have

\xi f-\xi f^{\prime}\in L_{\psi_{1}}
. The Bernstein-type inequality implies that, for all
v\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-v}
,

\displaystyle|M(f)-M(f^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=|\hat{\mathbf{P}}[(\xi f-\xi f^{\prime})-\mathbf{P}(\xi f-\xi f^{\prime})]|

(579)

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi f-\xi f^{\prime}-\mathbf{P}(\xi f-\xi f^{\prime})\|_{\psi_{1}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2v}{n}}+\frac{v}{n}\right).

(580)

Taking
v:=2^{k}+u
and using that
2^{\frac{k}{2}}\leq\sqrt{n}
, we get

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2v}{n}}+\frac{v}{n}\leq(1+\sqrt{2})\frac{2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{2u}{n}}+\frac{u}{n},

(581)

establishing the the first inequality claimed in the lemma.

Suppose now
2^{k/2}\geq\sqrt{n}
. The second inequality is proved in
[
37
]
, Lemma 5.4. We include the proof for completeness with slightly better constants. We first claim that, for any
v\geq 1
, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-nv)
,

\displaystyle\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{n}\leq[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{\psi_{2}}\sqrt{v}.

(582)

Indeed, by Bernstein’s inequality, for any
v\geq 1
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-nv}
,

\displaystyle|\hat{\mathbf{P}}[(f-f^{\prime})^{2}-\mathbf{P}(f-f^{\prime})^{2}]|\leq\|(f-f^{\prime})^{2}-\mathbf{P}(f-f^{\prime})^{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}\left(\sqrt{2v}+v\right)\leq 2(1+\sqrt{2})\|(f-f^{\prime})^{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}v.

(583)

For
v\geq 1
, we also have
|\mathbf{P}(f-f^{\prime})^{2}|\leq\|(f-f^{\prime})^{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\|(f-f^{\prime})^{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}v
. This, triangle inequality,
\|(f-f^{\prime})^{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}=\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}
and the display imply the claim.

Now, since
2^{k/2}\geq\sqrt{n}
, setting
v:=2^{k}u\frac{1}{n}\geq 1
we get
\exp(-nv)=\exp(-2^{k}u)
, entailing the claim.
∎

The following result is a straightforward modification of Lemma A.4 by Dirksen
[
37
]
.

Lemma 39
.

Fix
1\leq p<\infty
,
u\geq 2
and set
\ell:=\lfloor\log_{2}p\rfloor
. For every
n>\ell
let
(\Omega_{i}^{(k)})_{i\in I_{k}}
be a collection of events satisfying

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{i}^{(k)})\leq 2\exp(-(2^{k}+u)),\quad\mbox{for all $i\in I_{k}$}

(584)

or

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{i}^{(k)})\leq 2\exp(-2^{k}u),\quad\mbox{for all $i\in I_{k}$}.

(585)

If
|I_{k}|\leq 2^{2^{k}+1}
, then for an absolute constant
c>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\cup_{k>\ell}\cup_{i\in I_{k}}\Omega^{(k)}_{i}\right)\leq c\exp(-pu/4).

(586)

Proof of Theorem
6
.

Let
(F_{k})
be an optimal admissible sequences for

\gamma_{2}(F)
. Let
(\mathcal{F}_{k})
be defined by
\mathcal{F}_{0}:=F_{0}
and
\mathcal{F}_{k}:=\cup_{j\leq k}F_{j}
so that
|\mathcal{F}_{k}|\leq 2|F_{k}|=2^{2^{k}+1}
. Set
k_{0}:=\min\{k\geq 1:2^{k/2}>\sqrt{n}\}
and let us define

\mathcal{I}:=\{k\in\mathbb{N}:\ell<k<k_{0}\}

and

\mathcal{J}:=\{k\in\mathbb{N}:k\geq k_{0}\}
.
Given
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
f\in F
, let

\Pi_{k}(f)\in\argmin_{f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}}\mathsf{d}(f,f^{\prime}),

Given
f\in F
, we take some
\Pi_{0}(f)\in F
and for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
, we define the “lazy walk” chain selection by:

\displaystyle k_{j}(f):=\inf\left\{j\geq k_{j-1}(f):\mathsf{d}(f,\Pi_{j}(f))\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{d}(f,\Pi_{k_{j-1}(f)}(f))\right\}.

(587)

For simplicity of notation, we will set
\pi_{j}(f):=\Pi_{k_{j}(f)}(f)
. For
f\in F
, our proof will rely on the chain:

\displaystyle M(f)-M(\pi_{0}(f))

\displaystyle=\sum_{j:k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{J}}\left[M(\pi_{j+1}(f))-M(\pi_{j}(f))\right]+\sum_{j:k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{I}}\left[M(\pi_{j}(f))-M(\pi_{j-1}(f))\right],

(588)

where we have used that
\cup_{k\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{k}
is dense on
F
.

Fix
u\geq 1
. Given any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, define the event

\Omega_{k,\mathcal{I},u}
for which, for all
f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle|M(f)-M(f^{\prime})|\leq\left[(1+\sqrt{2})\frac{2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{2u}{n}}+\frac{u}{n}\right]\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{\psi_{2}}.

(589)

Define also the event
\Omega_{k,\mathcal{J},u}
for which, for all
f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{n}

\displaystyle\leq(\sqrt{u}+2^{k/2})\frac{[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{\psi_{2}}.

(590)

For simplicity, define the vector

\boldsymbol{\xi}:=(\xi_{i})_{i\in[n]}
and
\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{n}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{2}
. Given
v\geq 1
, we define the event

\Omega_{\xi,v}
, for which

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{n}\leq[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}\sqrt{v}.

(591)

By an union bound over all possible pairs

(\pi_{k-1}(f),\pi_{k}(f))
we have
|\Omega_{k,\mathcal{I},u}|\leq|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}||\mathcal{F}_{k}|\leq 2^{2^{k+1}}
. If

\Omega_{\mathcal{I},u}:=\cap_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\Omega_{k,\mathcal{I},u}
, the first bound on Lemma
38
for
k\in\mathcal{I}
and Lemma
39
imply that there is universal constant
c>0

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{I},u}^{c})\leq ce^{-u/4}.

Similarly, the second bound in Lemma
38
for
k\in\mathcal{J}
and Lemma
39
imply that for the event

\Omega_{\mathcal{J},u}:=\cap_{k\in\mathcal{J}}\Omega_{k,\mathcal{J},u}
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{J},u}^{c})\leq ce^{-u/4}.

Using Bernstein’s inequality for
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
we get
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\xi,v}^{c})\leq ce^{-vn}
. Hence, the event

\Omega_{u,v}:=\Omega_{\mathcal{I},u}\cap\Omega_{\mathcal{J},u}\cap\Omega_{\xi,v}
has

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{u,v}^{c})\leq ce^{-u/4}+ce^{-vn}
.

We next fix
u\geq 2
and
v\geq 1
and assume that
\Omega_{u,v}
always holds. We now bound the chain over
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
separately.

Part 1:
The subgaussian path
\mathcal{I}
.

Given
j
such that
k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{I}
, since
\pi_{j}(f),\pi_{j-1}(f)\in\mathcal{F}_{k_{j}(f)}
, we may apply (
589
) to
k:=k_{j}(f)
so that

\displaystyle|M(\pi_{j}(f))-M(\pi_{j-1}(f))|\leq\left[(1+\sqrt{2})\frac{2^{k_{j}(f)/2}}{\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{2u}{n}}+\frac{u}{n}\right]\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}\|\pi_{j}(f)-\pi_{j-1}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}}.

(592)

We note that, by triangle inequality and minimality of
k_{j-1}(f)
,

\displaystyle\|\pi_{j}(f)-\pi_{j-1}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\mathsf{d}(f,\mathcal{F}_{k_{j}(f)})+\mathsf{d}(f,\mathcal{F}_{k_{j-1}(f)})\leq\mathsf{d}(f,\mathcal{F}_{k_{j}(f)})+2\mathsf{d}(f,\mathcal{F}_{k_{j}(f)-1}),

(593)

so that

\displaystyle\sum_{j:k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{I}}2^{k_{j}(f)/2}\|\pi_{j}(f)-\pi_{j-1}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq(1+2\sqrt{2})\gamma_{2}(F).

(594)

Moreover, by the definition of the lazy walked chain and a geometric series bound,

\displaystyle\sum_{j:k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{I}}\|\pi_{j}(f)-\pi_{j-1}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq 4\mathsf{d}(f,\mathcal{F}_{0})\leq 4\bar{\Delta}(F).

(595)

We thus conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{j:k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{I}}[M(\pi_{j}(f))-M(\pi_{j-1}(f))]\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4\left(\sqrt{\frac{2u}{n}}+\frac{u}{n}\right)\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}\bar{\Delta}(F)+(1+\sqrt{2})(1+2\sqrt{2})\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}\frac{\gamma_{2}(F)}{\sqrt{n}}.

(596)

Part 2:
The subexponential path
\mathcal{J}
.

Let us denote by
\mathbf{Q}
the joint distribution of
(\xi,X)
and
\hat{\mathbf{Q}}
the empirical distribution associated to
\{(\xi_{i},X_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
. In particular,
M(f)=\hat{\mathbf{Q}}(\cdot)f-\mathbf{Q}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}(\cdot)f
. By Jensen’s and triangle inequalities,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{j:k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{J}}[M(\pi_{j+1}(f))-M(\pi_{j}(f))]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j:k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{J}}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}(\cdot)\left|\pi_{j+1}(f)-\pi_{j}(f)\right|

(597)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j:k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{J}}\mathbf{Q}\hat{\mathbf{Q}}(\cdot)\left|\pi_{j+1}(f)-\pi_{j}(f)\right|.

(598)

For convenience, we set
\hat{T}_{j}:=\hat{\mathbf{Q}}(\cdot)\left|\pi_{j+1}(f)-\pi_{j}(f)\right|
.

Given
j
such that
k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{J}
, since

\pi_{j+1}(f),\pi_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{F}_{k_{j+1}(f)}
, we may apply (
590
) to
k:=k_{j+1}(f)
. This fact, (
591
) and Cauchy-Schwarz yield

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{n}\|\pi_{j+1}(f)-\pi_{j}(f)\|_{n}\leq\sqrt{v}[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}(\sqrt{u}+2^{k_{j+1}(f)/2})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\|\pi_{j+1}(f)-\pi_{j}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}}.

(599)

In a similar fashion, we can also state that with probability at least
1-ce^{-u/4}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{T}_{j}}{\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{n}}\leq(\sqrt{u}+2^{k_{j+1}(f)/2})\frac{[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\pi_{j+1}(f)-\pi_{j}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}},

(600)

so integrating the tail leads to

\displaystyle\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\hat{T}_{j}}{\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{n}}\right)^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\leq c2^{k_{j+1}(f)/2}\frac{[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\pi_{j+1}(f)-\pi_{j}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}},

(601)

and by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\hat{T}_{j}\leq\left\{\mathbb{E}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{n}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\hat{T}_{j}}{\|\boldsymbol{\xi}\|_{n}}\right)^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\leq c[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}\frac{\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{2^{k_{j+1}(f)/2}\|\pi_{j+1}(f)-\pi_{j}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(602)

We thus conclude from (
598
) and a analogous reasoning to (
594
) and (
595
) that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{j:k_{j}(f)\in\mathcal{J}}[M(\pi_{j+1}(f))-M(\pi_{j}(f))]\right|

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}^{2}\sqrt{v}\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}\sqrt{u}\frac{4\bar{\Delta}(F)}{\sqrt{n}}

(603)

\displaystyle+\left(c_{1}^{2}\sqrt{v}+c_{1}\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}}(1+2\sqrt{2})\frac{\gamma_{2}(F)}{\sqrt{n}},

(604)

with
c_{1}:=[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}
.

From the above bound, (
596
) and (
588
) we conclude that, for any
u\geq 2
and
v\geq 1
, on the event
\Omega_{u,v}
of probability at least
1-ce^{-u/4}-ce^{-nv}
, we have the bound stated in the theorem.
∎

Appendix C
The Product Process

In this section we prove Theorem
7
— restate it here in a more general form. We refer to notation and definitions in Section
7
.

Theorem 40

(Product process)
.

Let
F,G
be subclasses of
L_{\psi_{2}}
. For any
1\leq p<\infty
,

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\,\sup_{(f,g)\in F\times G}|A(f,g)|\,\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{p}\lesssim\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(F)\gamma_{2,p}(G)}{n}+\bar{\Delta}(F)\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(G)}{\sqrt{n}}+\bar{\Delta}(G)\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(F)}{\sqrt{n}}+\bar{\Delta}(F)\bar{\Delta}(G)\left(\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\frac{p}{n}\right).

(605)

In particular, there exist universal constants
c,C>0
, such that for all
n\geq 1
and
u\geq 1
, with probability at least
1-e^{-u}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{(f,g)\in F\times G}\left|A(f,g)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\frac{\gamma_{2}(F)\gamma_{2}(G)}{n}+\bar{\Delta}(F)\frac{\gamma_{2}(G)}{\sqrt{n}}+\bar{\Delta}(G)\frac{\gamma_{2}(F)}{\sqrt{n}}\right]

(606)

\displaystyle+c\sup_{(f,g)\in F\times G}\|fg-\mathbf{P}fg\|_{\psi_{1}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{u}{n}}+\frac{u}{n}\right).

(607)

Before proving the theorem we will need some auxiliary results.

Lemma 41
.

Let
f,f^{\prime}
and
g,g^{\prime}
in
L_{\psi_{2}}
.

If for
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
2^{k/2}\leq\sqrt{n}
, then for any
u\geq 1
, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-2^{k}u)
,

\displaystyle|A(f,g)-A(f^{\prime},g^{\prime})|\leq 2(1+\sqrt{2})\frac{u2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\|fg-f^{\prime}g^{\prime}\|_{\psi_{1}}.

(608)

If for
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
2^{k/2}\geq\sqrt{n}
, then for any
u\geq 1
, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-2^{k}u)
,

\displaystyle\|f-f^{\prime}\|_{n}\leq\sqrt{u}2^{k/2}\frac{[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{d}(f,f^{\prime}).

(609)

Proof.

The proof is as in the proof of Lemma
38
using Bernstein’s inequality. A minor difference is that it is used the larger scaling
v:=2^{k}u
for all
u\geq 1
instead of
v:=2^{k}+u
for all
u>0
. The fact that

\sqrt{2u}+u\leq(\sqrt{2}+1)u
for
u\geq 1
is used in the first inequality of the lemma.
∎

As in the proof of Theorem
6
, we combine Dirksen’s method
[
37
]
with Talagrand’s
[
83
]
“lazy-walked” chain. One difference now is that we will explicitly need Dirksen’s bound for the quadratic process

A(f):=\hat{\mathbf{P}}(f^{2}-\mathbf{P}f^{2}).

The following proposition is a corollary of the proof of Theorem 5.5 in
[
37
]
.

Proposition 9

(Dirksen
[
37
]
, Theorem 5.5)
.

Let
F\subset L_{\psi_{2}}
. Given
1\leq p<\infty
, set
\ell:=\lfloor\log_{2}p\rfloor
and
k_{0}:=\min\{k>\ell:2^{k/2}>\sqrt{n}\}
. Let
(\mathcal{F}_{k})
be an optimal admissible sequence for
\gamma_{2,p}(F,\mathsf{d})
and, for any
f\in F
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\pi_{k}(f)\in\argmin_{f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell}}\mathsf{d}(f,f^{\prime})
. Then there exists universal constant
c>0
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
u\geq 2
, with probability at least
1-ce^{-pu/4}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in F}\sup_{k\geq k_{0}}\left|A(\pi_{k}(f))\right|^{1/2}-\sup_{f\in F}\left|A(\pi_{k_{0}}(f))\right|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{u}\left[25\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(F,\mathsf{d})}{\sqrt{n}}+\left(85\frac{\bar{\Delta}(F)\gamma_{2,p}(F,\mathsf{d})}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{1/2}\right].

(610)

Moreover, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
u\geq 1
, with probability at least
1-ce^{-pu/4}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in F}\left|A(\pi_{k_{0}}(f))\right|^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{u}[4(1+\sqrt{2})+2]^{1/2}\bar{\Delta}(F).

(611)

Finally, besides Lemma
39
, we need some additional lemmas from Dirksen
[
37
]
.

Lemma 42

(Dirksen
[
37
]
, Lemma A.3)
.

Fix
1\leq p<\infty
, set
\ell:=\lfloor\log_{2}p\rfloor
and let
(X_{t})_{t\in T}
be a finite collection of real-valued random variables with
|T|\leq 2^{2^{\ell}}
.

Then

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{t\in T}|X_{t}|^{p}\right)^{1/p}\leq 2\sup_{t\in T}(\mathbb{E}|X_{t}|^{p})^{1/p}.

(612)

Lemma 43

(Dirksen
[
37
]
, Lemma A.5)
.

Fix
1\leq p<\infty
and
0<\alpha<\infty
. Let
\gamma\geq 0
and suppose that
\xi
is a positive random variable such that for some
c\geq 1
and
u_{*}>0
, for all
u\geq u_{*}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\xi>\gamma u)\leq c\exp(-pu^{\alpha}/4).

(613)

Then, for a constant
\tilde{c}_{\alpha}>0
, depending only on
\alpha
,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}\xi^{p})^{1/p}\leq\gamma(\tilde{c}_{\alpha}c+u_{*}).

(614)

Lemma 44

(Dirksen
[
37
]
, Lemma A.2)
.

Let
0<\alpha<\infty
. If a random variable
X
satisfies, for some
a_{1},a_{2}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|X|\geq a_{1}u+a_{2}\sqrt{u})\leq\exp(-u),

(615)

for all
u\geq 0
, then, for absolute constant
C>0
, for all
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}|X|^{p})^{1/p}\leq C(a_{1}p+a_{2}\sqrt{p}).

(616)

Proof of Theorem
40
.

Let
(\mathcal{F}_{k})
and
(\mathcal{G}_{k})
be optimal admissible sequences for
\gamma_{2,p}(F)
and

\gamma_{2,p}(G)
respectively. Set
\ell:=\lfloor\log_{2}p\rfloor
,
k_{0}:=\min\{k>\ell:2^{k/2}>\sqrt{n}\}
and let us define

\mathcal{I}:=\{k\in\mathbb{N}:\ell<k<k_{0}\}

and

\mathcal{J}:=\{k\in\mathbb{N}:k\geq k_{0}\}
. Given
(f,g)\in F\times G
, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we take the usual selections

\pi_{k}(f)\in\argmin_{f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}}\mathsf{d}(f,f^{\prime}),

and

\Pi_{k}(g)\in\argmin_{g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}\mathsf{d}(g,g^{\prime}).

For convenience, we also define

\mathcal{P}_{k}(f,g):=A(\pi_{k}(f),\Pi_{k}(g))
and

\mathscr{P}_{k}(f,g):=\pi_{k}(f)\Pi_{k}(g)
. Our proof will rely on the chain:

\displaystyle A(f,g)-A(\pi_{\ell}(f),\Pi_{\ell}(g))

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{J}}\left[\mathcal{P}_{k+1}(f,g)-\mathcal{P}_{k}(f,g)\right]+\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\left[\mathcal{P}_{k}(f,g)-\mathcal{P}_{k-1}(f,g)\right],

(617)

where we have used that
\cup_{k\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{k}\times\mathcal{G}_{k}
is dense on
F\times G
.

Fix
u\geq 2
. Given any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, define the event

\Omega_{k,\mathcal{I},u,p}
for which, for all
f\in F
and
g\in G
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{P}_{k}(f,g)-\mathcal{P}_{k-1}(f,g)|\leq 2(1+\sqrt{2})u2^{k/2}\frac{\|\mathscr{P}_{k}(f,g)-\mathscr{P}_{k-1}(f,g)]\|_{\psi_{1}}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(618)

Define also the event
\Omega_{k,\mathcal{J},u,p}
for which, for all
f\in F
and
g\in G
, we have both inequalities:

\displaystyle\|\pi_{k+1}(f)-\pi_{k}(f)\|_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{u}2^{k/2}\frac{[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\pi_{k+1}(f)-\pi_{k}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}},

(619)

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{k+1}(g)-\Pi_{k}(g)\|_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{u}2^{k/2}\frac{[2(1+\sqrt{2})+1]^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\Pi_{k+1}(g)-\Pi_{k}(g)\|_{\psi_{2}}.

(620)

By an union bound over all possible 4-tuples

(\pi_{k-1}(f),\pi_{k}(f),\Pi_{k-1}(g),\Pi_{k}(g))
we have that
|\Omega_{k,\mathcal{I},u,p}|\leq|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}||\mathcal{F}_{k}||\mathcal{G}_{k}||\mathcal{G}_{k-1}|\leq 2^{2^{k+2}}
. If
\Omega_{\mathcal{I},u,p}:=\cap_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\Omega_{k,\mathcal{I},u,p}
, the first bound on Lemma
41
for
k\in\mathcal{I}
and Lemma
39
(using that
k>\ell
over
\mathcal{I}
) imply that there is universal constant
c>0

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{I},u,p}^{c})\leq ce^{-pu/4}.

Similarly, the second bound in Lemma
41
for
k\in\mathcal{J}
and Lemma
39
(using that
k>\ell
over
\mathcal{J}
) imply that for the event

\Omega_{\mathcal{J},u,p}:=\cap_{k\in\mathcal{J}}\Omega_{k,\mathcal{J},u,p}
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{J},u,p}^{c})\leq ce^{-pu/4}.

We now also define the event
\Omega_{u,p}
as the intersection of
\Omega_{\mathcal{I},u,p}\cap\Omega_{\mathcal{J},u,p}
and the events for which both inequalities of Proposition
9
hold for both classes
F
and
G
. Clearly, by such proposition and the two previous displays we have

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{u,p}^{c})\leq ce^{-pu/4}
from an union bound.

We next fix
u\geq 2
and assume that
\Omega_{u,p}
always holds. We now bound the chain over
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
separately.

Part 1:
The subgaussian path
\mathcal{I}
.

From (
618
) and the inequality

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{P}_{k}(f,g)-\mathscr{P}_{k-1}(f,g)\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\pi_{k}(f)-\pi_{k-1}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}}\|\Pi_{k}(g)\|_{\psi_{2}}+\|\pi_{k-1}(f)\|_{\psi_{2}}\|\Pi_{k}(g)-\Pi_{k-1}(g)\|_{\psi_{2}}

(621)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\Delta}(G)[\mathsf{d}(f,\pi_{k}(f))+\mathsf{d}(f,\pi_{k-1}(f))]+\bar{\Delta}(F)[\mathsf{d}(g,\Pi_{k}(g))+\mathsf{d}(g,\Pi_{k-1}(g))]

(622)

implying

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathcal{P}_{k}(f,g)-\mathcal{P}_{k-1}(f,g)]\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2(1+\sqrt{2})^{2}\frac{u}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\bar{\Delta}(F)\gamma_{2,p}(G)+\bar{\Delta}(G)\gamma_{2,p}(F)\right].

(623)

Part 2:
The subexponential path
\mathcal{J}
.

Note that
A(f,g)=\hat{\mathbf{P}}fg-\mathbf{P}(\hat{\mathbf{P}}fg)
and thus, by Jensen’s and triangle inequalities,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{J}}[\mathcal{P}_{k+1}(f,g)-\mathcal{P}_{k}(f,g)]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{J}}\hat{\mathbf{P}}[\mathscr{P}_{k+1}(f,g)-\mathscr{P}_{k}(f,g)]\right|+\left|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{J}}\mathbf{P}\hat{\mathbf{P}}[\mathscr{P}_{k+1}(f,g)-\mathscr{P}_{k}(f,g)]\right|

(624)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathcal{J}}\hat{\mathbf{P}}\left|\mathscr{P}_{k+1}(f,g)-\mathscr{P}_{k}(f,g)\right|+\sum_{k\in\mathcal{J}}\mathbf{P}\hat{\mathbf{P}}\left|\mathscr{P}_{k+1}(f,g)-\mathscr{P}_{k}(f,g)\right|.

(625)

Let us denote
\hat{T}_{k}:=\hat{\mathbf{P}}\left|\mathscr{P}_{k+1}(f,g)-\mathscr{P}_{k}(f,g)\right|
. We have the split

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\hat{\mathbf{P}}\pi_{k}(f)[\Pi_{k+1}(g)-\Pi_{k}(g)]\right|+\left|\hat{\mathbf{P}}\Pi_{k+1}(g)[\pi_{k+1}(f)-\pi_{k}(f)]\right|.

(626)

By Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\left|\hat{\mathbf{P}}\pi_{k}(f)[\Pi_{k+1}(g)-\Pi_{k}(g)]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\pi_{k}(f)\|_{n}\|\Pi_{k+1}(g)-\Pi_{k}(g)]\|_{n}

(627)

\displaystyle\leq\left[\{A(\pi_{k}(f))\}^{1/2}+\{\mathbf{P}\pi_{k}^{2}(f)\}^{1/2}\right]\|\Pi_{k+1}(g)-\Pi_{k}(g)]\|_{n},

(628)

which together with (
620
), bounds in Proposition
9
and
\sqrt{u}\geq 1
give

\displaystyle\left|\hat{\mathbf{P}}\pi_{k}(f)[\Pi_{k+1}(g)-\Pi_{k}(g)]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{2}u2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left[c_{1}\bar{\Delta}(F)+25\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(F)}{\sqrt{n}}+\left\{\frac{85\bar{\Delta}(F)\gamma_{2,p}(F)}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}^{1/2}\right]\mathsf{d}(\Pi_{k+1}(g),\Pi_{k}(g))

(629)

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}\frac{u2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{d}(\Pi_{k+1}(g),\Pi_{k}(g))\left[c_{3}\bar{\Delta}(F)+c_{4}\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(F)}{\sqrt{n}}\right],

(630)

by Young’s inequality and constants
c_{1}:=\{4(1+\sqrt{2}+2)\}^{1/2}+1
,
c_{2}:=\{2(1+\sqrt{2}+1)\}^{1/2}
,
c_{3}:=c_{1}+\frac{\sqrt{85}}{2}
and
c_{4}:=25+\frac{\sqrt{85}}{2}
.
An identical bound gives

\displaystyle\left|\hat{\mathbf{P}}\Pi_{k+1}(g)[\pi_{k+1}(f)-\pi_{k}(f)]\right|

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}\frac{u2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{d}(\pi_{k+1}(f),\pi_{k}(f))\left[c_{3}\bar{\Delta}(G)+c_{4}\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(G)}{\sqrt{n}}\right].

(631)

We thus conclude that

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}\frac{u2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{d}(\Pi_{k+1}(g),\Pi_{k}(g))\left[c_{3}\bar{\Delta}(F)+c_{4}\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(F)}{\sqrt{n}}\right]

(632)

\displaystyle+c_{2}\frac{u2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{d}(\pi_{k+1}(f),\pi_{k}(f))\left[c_{3}\bar{\Delta}(G)+c_{4}\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(G)}{\sqrt{n}}\right].

(633)

Note that, in fact, we have proved that the above bound on
\hat{T}_{k}
holds with probability at least
1-c\exp(-pu/4)
for any
u\geq 2
. Thus, from Lemma
43
we have, for some universal constant
c_{0}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\hat{T}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}c_{2}\frac{u2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{d}(\Pi_{k+1}(g),\Pi_{k}(g))\left[c_{3}\bar{\Delta}(F)+c_{4}\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(F)}{\sqrt{n}}\right]

(634)

\displaystyle+c_{0}c_{2}\frac{u2^{k/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\mathsf{d}(\pi_{k+1}(f),\pi_{k}(f))\left[c_{3}\bar{\Delta}(G)+c_{4}\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(G)}{\sqrt{n}}\right].

(635)

Using the previous two bounds in (
625
) gives, after using the triangle inequality for
\mathsf{d}
, summing over
k\in\mathcal{J}
and using the definition of
\gamma_{2,p}(F)
and
\gamma_{2,p}(G)
(recalling that
k>\ell
),

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{J}}[\mathcal{P}_{k+1}(f,g)-\mathcal{P}_{k}(f,g)]\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1+c_{0})(1+2^{-1/2})c_{2}\frac{u}{\sqrt{n}}\gamma_{2,p}(G)\left[c_{3}\bar{\Delta}(F)+c_{4}\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(F)}{\sqrt{n}}\right]

(636)

\displaystyle+(1+c_{0})(1+2^{-1/2})c_{2}\frac{u}{\sqrt{n}}\gamma_{2,p}(F)\left[c_{3}\bar{\Delta}(G)+c_{4}\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(G)}{\sqrt{n}}\right].

(637)

From the above bound, (
623
) and (
617
) we conclude that, for any
u\geq 2
, on the event
\Omega_{u,p}
of probability at least
1-e^{-pu/4}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{(f,g)\in F\times G}|A(f,g)|^{1/2}-\sup_{(f,g)\in F\times G}|A(\pi_{\ell}(f),\Pi_{\ell}(g))|^{1/2}

(638)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{u}\left[\frac{c_{5}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\bar{\Delta}(F)\gamma_{2,p}(G)+\bar{\Delta}(G)\gamma_{2,p}(F)\right)+c_{6}\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(F)\gamma_{2,p}(G)}{n}\right]^{1/2},

(639)

with
c_{5}:=2(1+\sqrt{2}^{2}+(1+c_{0})(1+2^{-1/2}))c_{2}c_{3}
and

c_{6}:=2(1+c_{0})c_{2}c_{4}(1+2^{-1/2})
. This and Lemma
43
(with
\alpha=2
) imply that

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\,\sup_{(f,g)\in F\times G}|A(f,g)|^{1/2}-\sup_{(f,g)\in F\times G}|A(\pi_{\ell}(f),\Pi_{\ell}(g))|^{1/2}\,\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{p}

(640)

\displaystyle\leq c\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\bar{\Delta}(F)\gamma_{2,p}(G)+\bar{\Delta}(G)\gamma_{2,p}(F)\right)+\frac{\gamma_{2,p}(F)\gamma_{2,p}(G)}{n}\right]^{1/2}.

(641)

We also have from Lemma
42
,

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{(f,g)\in F\times G}|A(\pi_{\ell}(f),\Pi_{\ell}(g))|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sup_{(f,g)\in F\times G}\left(\mathbb{E}|A(\pi_{\ell}(f),\Pi_{\ell}(g))|^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}

(642)

\displaystyle\leq c\sup_{(f,g)\in F\times G}\left[\|\mathscr{P}_{\ell}(f,g)-\mathbf{P}\mathscr{P}_{\ell}(f,g)\|_{\psi_{1}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\frac{p}{n}\right)\right],

(643)

where the second inequality follows from Bernstein’s inequality for
A(\pi_{\ell}(f),\Pi_{\ell}(g))
and Lemma
44
. The two previous displays finish the proof.
∎
